\displaystyle\langle\xi^{+}_{n,1}\xi^{+}_{n,2}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\cal{D}}_{k}^{f}(m){g^{2}\over 4}{-i\omega_{n}-\Re m\over\omega_{n}^{2}+|m|^{2}},~\langle\xi^{-}_{n,1}\xi^{-}_{n,2}\rangle={\cal{D}}_{k}^{f}(m){g^{2}\over 4}{i\omega_{n}-\Re m\over\omega_{n}^{2}+|m|^{2}},

as well as for the opposite-sign correlators,

\displaystyle\langle\xi^{+}_{n,1}\xi^{-}_{n,2}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\cal{D}}_{k}^{f}(m){g^{2}\over 4}{-i\Im m\over\omega_{n}^{2}+|m|^{2}},~\langle\xi^{-}_{n,1}\xi^{+}_{n,2}\rangle={\cal{D}}_{k}^{f}(m){g^{2}\over 4}{-i\Im m\over\omega_{n}^{2}+|m|^{2}},

(276)

In order to read off the components of the propagator from (
273
) (again omitting the
\otimes
sign),

\displaystyle\langle\Psi(x)\otimes\bar{\Psi}(y)\rangle=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\langle\lambda_{\alpha}(x)\otimes\lambda^{\beta}(y)\rangle&\langle\lambda_{\alpha}(x)\otimes\bar{\lambda}_{\dot{\beta}}(y)\rangle\cr\langle\bar{\lambda}^{\dot{\alpha}}(x)\otimes\lambda^{\beta}(y)\rangle&\langle\bar{\lambda}^{\dot{\alpha}}(x)\otimes\bar{\lambda}_{\dot{\beta}}(y)\rangle\end{array}\right)~,

the last piece of information, after (
C
,
276
), needed are the products of
\Sigma,\bar{\Sigma}
functions entering (
273
), still keeping as much generality as possible.

To this end, we recall the expressions for
\Sigma^{\pm}
and
\bar{\Sigma}^{\pm}
from (
C
) and (
255
). Then, we find for the inner products appearing in (
273
)

\displaystyle\left[\Sigma_{n,1}^{+}(x)\otimes\bar{\Sigma}_{n,2}^{+}(y)-\Sigma_{n,2}^{+}(x)\otimes\bar{\Sigma}_{n,1}^{+}(y)\right]

(280)

\displaystyle=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}(\sigma_{\mu\;\alpha 1}\sigma_{\nu\gamma 2}-\sigma_{\mu\;\alpha 2}\sigma_{\nu\gamma 2})\epsilon^{\gamma\beta}{D_{\mu}\phi_{n}(x)\otimes D_{\nu}\phi_{n}(y)\over\omega_{n}^{2}}&(\sigma_{\mu\;\alpha 1}\epsilon_{2\dot{\beta}}-\sigma_{\mu\;\alpha 2}\epsilon_{1\dot{\beta}}){iD_{\mu}\phi_{n}(x)\otimes\phi_{n}(y)\over\omega_{n}}\cr(\delta^{\dot{\alpha}1}\sigma_{\nu\;\gamma 2}-\delta^{\dot{\alpha}2}\sigma_{\nu\;\gamma 1})\epsilon^{\gamma\beta}{i\phi_{n}(x)\otimes D_{\nu}\phi_{n}(y)\over\omega_{n}}&(\delta^{\dot{\alpha}1}\epsilon_{\dot{\beta}2}-\delta^{\dot{\alpha}2}\epsilon_{\dot{\beta}1})\phi_{n}(x)\otimes\phi_{n}(y)\end{array}\right),

With some minor matrix manipulations, this can be cast into the friendlier form:

\displaystyle\left[\Sigma_{n,1}^{+}(x)\otimes\bar{\Sigma}_{n,2}^{+}(y)-\Sigma_{n,2}^{+}(x)\otimes\bar{\Sigma}_{n,1}^{+}(y)\right]=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}-(\sigma_{\mu}\bar{\sigma}_{\nu})_{\alpha}^{\;\;\beta}{D_{\mu}\phi_{n}(x)\otimes D_{\nu}\phi_{n}(y)\over\omega_{n}^{2}}&~\sigma_{\mu\;\alpha\dot{\beta}}\;{iD_{\mu}\phi_{n}(x)\otimes\phi_{n}(y)\over\omega_{n}}\cr-\bar{\sigma}_{\nu}^{\;\dot{\alpha}\beta}\;{i\phi_{n}(x)\otimes D_{\nu}\phi_{n}(y)\over\omega_{n}}&-\delta^{\dot{\alpha}}_{\dot{\beta}}\;\phi_{n}(x)\otimes\phi_{n}(y)\end{array}\right).

We now notice that in the
11
entry above, we can use

\displaystyle\sigma_{\mu}\bar{\sigma}_{\nu}D_{\mu}\phi_{n}(x)\otimes D_{\nu}\phi_{n}(y)=D_{\mu}\phi_{n}(x)\otimes D_{\mu}\phi_{n}(y)+\sigma_{\mu\nu}(D_{\mu}\phi_{n}(x)\otimes D_{\nu}\phi_{n}(y)-D_{\nu}\phi_{n}(x)\otimes D_{\mu}\phi_{n}(y)),

but at this point, without explicit expressions for
\phi_{n}
, there is no general argument for the antisymmetric term vanishing (however, the antisymmetric term vanishes for
x=y
, which is sufficient for the computation of the gaugino condensate).

Next, we turn to the same product as (
C
), but involving
\Sigma^{-}
instead. We notice that because of (
C
) and (
255
), it has the same diagonal entries as (
C
), but opposite-sign off-diagonal entries. Thus, we can immediately write

\displaystyle\left[\Sigma_{n,1}^{-}(x)\otimes\bar{\Sigma}_{n,2}^{-}(y)-\Sigma_{n,2}^{-}(x)\otimes\bar{\Sigma}_{n,1}^{-}(y)\right]=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}-(\sigma_{\mu}\bar{\sigma}_{\nu})_{\alpha}^{\;\;\beta}{D_{\mu}\phi_{n}(x)\otimes D_{\nu}\phi_{n}(y)\over\omega_{n}^{2}}&~-\sigma_{\mu\;\alpha\dot{\beta}}\;{iD_{\mu}\phi_{n}(x)\otimes\phi_{n}(y)\over\omega_{n}}\cr\bar{\sigma}_{\nu}^{\;\dot{\alpha}\beta}\;{i\phi_{n}(x)\otimes D_{\nu}\phi_{n}(y)\over\omega_{n}}&-\delta^{\dot{\alpha}}_{\dot{\beta}}\;\phi_{n}(x)\ \otimes\phi_{n}(y)\end{array}\right).

Finally, we turn to the mixed products multiplying the
\Im m\neq 0
correlators in (
276
,
273
). In view of the equality of the correllators,
\langle\xi_{n,1}^{+}\xi_{n,2}^{-}\rangle=\langle\xi_{n,1}^{-}\xi_{n,2}^{+}\rangle
, as per (
276
), we need only the expression below:

\displaystyle\left[\Sigma_{n,s}^{+}(x)\otimes\bar{\Sigma}_{n,s^{\prime}}^{-}(y)\epsilon^{ss^{\prime}}+\Sigma_{n,s}^{-}(x)\otimes\bar{\Sigma}_{n,s^{\prime}}^{+}(y)\epsilon^{ss^{\prime}}\right]=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}2(\sigma_{\mu}\bar{\sigma}_{\nu})_{\alpha}^{\;\;\beta}\;{D_{\mu}\phi_{n}(x)\otimes D_{\nu}\phi_{n}(y)\over\omega_{n}^{2}}&0\cr 0&-2\delta^{\dot{\alpha}}_{\dot{\beta}}\;\phi_{n}(x)\otimes\phi_{n}(y)\end{array}\right).

We also need the outer product of the zero mode wavefunctions that enter (
273
). From (
C
,
256
), we find:

\displaystyle\left[\Sigma^{(0)}_{p,1}(x)\otimes\bar{\Sigma}^{(0)}_{q,2}(y)-\Sigma^{(0)}_{p,2}(x)\otimes\bar{\Sigma}^{(0)}_{q,1}(y)\right]=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{k}\left(\psi^{(0)}_{\alpha p,1}(x)\otimes\psi^{(0)\beta}_{\;\;\;p,2}(y)-\psi^{(0)}_{\alpha p,2}(x)\otimes\psi^{(0)\beta}_{\;\;\;p,1}(y)\right)&~0\cr 0&~0\end{array}\right).

Finally, substituting into (
273
) the expression for the Grassmann correllators (
274
,
C
,
276
) and those for the outer products of the wave functions (
C
,
C
,
C
,
C
), we obtain the expression:

\displaystyle\langle\Psi(x)\otimes\bar{\Psi}(y)\rangle_{unnorm.}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\langle\lambda_{\alpha}(x)\otimes\lambda^{\beta}(y)\rangle&\langle\lambda_{\alpha}(x)\otimes\bar{\lambda}_{\dot{\beta}}(y)\rangle\cr\langle\bar{\lambda}^{\dot{\alpha}}(x)\otimes\lambda^{\beta}(y)\rangle&\langle\bar{\lambda}^{\dot{\alpha}}(x)\otimes\bar{\lambda}_{\dot{\beta}}(y)\rangle\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\cal{D}}_{k}^{f}(m){g^{2}\over 2}\left\{\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}{m^{*}\over|m|^{2}}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{k}\left(\psi^{(0)}_{\alpha p,1}(x)\otimes\psi^{(0)\beta}_{\;\;\;p,2}(y)-\psi^{(0)}_{\alpha p,2}(x)\otimes\psi^{(0)\beta}_{\;\;\;p,1}(y)\right)&~0\cr 0&~0\end{array}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.~~~~~+\sum_{n}\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}{m^{*}\over\omega_{n}^{2}+|m|^{2}}\;(\sigma_{\mu}\bar{\sigma}_{\nu})_{\alpha}^{\;\;\beta}\;{D_{\mu}\phi_{n}(x)\otimes D_{\nu}\phi_{n}(y)\over\omega_{n}^{2}}&{\sigma_{\mu\;\alpha\dot{\beta}}\over\omega_{n}^{2}+|m|^{2}}\;{D_{\mu}\phi_{n}(x)\otimes\phi_{n}(y)}\cr-{\bar{\sigma}_{\nu}^{\dot{\alpha}\beta}\over\omega_{n}^{2}+|m|^{2}}\;{\phi_{n}(x)\otimes D_{\nu}\phi_{n}(y)}&{m\over\omega_{n}^{2}+|m|^{2}}\;\delta^{\dot{\alpha}}_{\dot{\beta}}\;\phi_{n}(x)\otimes\phi_{n}(y)\end{array}\right)\right\}.

Comments:

1.

Eqn. (
C
) is the general expression propagator in a
Q=k/N
self dual background under the assumption of
2k
dotted fermion zero modes saturating the index (this can be relaxed, see below). We recall again that the
{\cal{D}}_{k}^{f}(m)
factor, given in (
271
), is due to the fact that the correlator is assumed to be unnormalized. Furthermore, all Grassmann ordering ambiguity is contained in this factor (as explicitly outlined in (
269
)).

2.

The propagator is expressed in terms of sums over the zero and nonzero modes in the instanton background:
In writing the zero mode contribution to the propagator, we allowed the zero mode wave-functions to be
x
-dependent, as is the case at the subleading orders in the
\Delta
-expansion in the fractional instanton background.

3.

The sum over nonzero modes is expressed in terms of
\phi_{n}
, the nonzero mode eigenfunctions of the adjoint scalar Laplace operator (
246
), in a self-dual background of charge
Q=k/N
, normalized as in (
248
); thus
\phi_{n}
are taken Hermitean. Self-duality of
F_{\mu\nu}
was essential to find the action in the diagonal form (
267
) (it was used e.g. in deriving (
260
)).

Due to the nature of the adjoint path integral over
\Psi
, with
\bar{\Psi}=\Psi^{t}C
, no complex conjugates of
\phi_{n}
appear in the path integral and in the propagators (as in Sec. B.2 in
Anber:2022qsz
).
The orthonormality of
\phi_{n}
(
248
) was essential in the derivation of the action (
267
), and the consequent expression for the propagator, but completeness in the form (
247
) was not used. Thus, should one study a background that also has dotted zero modes (which would change the Laplacian completeness condition) these can simply be added to the above propagator.

4.

All products of wave functions and their derivatives appearing in (
C
) should be understood as outer products, explicitly the l.h.s. and r.h.s. should be understood as

\displaystyle\langle\lambda_{\alpha}(x)\otimes\lambda^{\beta}(y)\rangle

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle\langle\lambda_{ij\;\alpha}(x)\lambda_{kl}^{\beta}(y)\rangle,

\displaystyle D_{\mu}\phi_{n}(x)\otimes\phi_{n}(y)

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle(D_{\mu}\phi_{n})_{ij}(x)\;\phi_{n\;kl}(y),~\text{etc.}

(310)

Throughout, we suppressed the adjoint indices,
i,j,k,l=1,...N
; these can be restored in (
C
), following (
4
).

These indices can be further split into
SU(\ell)

(C,B,..)
and
SU(k)

(C^{\prime},B^{\prime},...)
indices. This is necessary when one develops explicit expressions for
\phi_{n}
and the propagators by solving for the fractional instanton Laplacian eigenfunctions in the
\Delta
-expansion, as we describe below.

5.

All expressions in (
C
) are valid in an exactly self-dual background, assumed to be “generic,” i.e. such that the Laplacian (
246
) has no zero modes. We have no exact expression for such a background. However, within the
\Delta
expansion, we know the background as a series expansion in
\Delta
, see (
2.3
).
Thus, in (
C
), all terms should be understood using the same
\Delta
-expansion: the eigenvalues of the Laplacian
\omega_{n}
, its eigenfunctions
\phi_{n}
, the background entering the covariant derivative (
D_{\mu}=\partial+i[A_{\mu},..]
), as well as the zero mode wavefunctions
\psi_{\alpha p,i}^{(0)}
are all given as an expansion in
\Delta
.

In the following sections, we evaluate the terms in the propagator to order
\Delta^{0}
. However, we note that there are
k
eigenvalues of the Laplacian of order
\Delta^{1}
, whose leading contribution to the propagator is of order
1\over\Delta
in the
|m|L\ll\sqrt{\Delta}
limit (see discussion near (
335
) below), which we also evaluate.

Appendix D
The
Q={k\over N}
propagator in the diagonal
SU(k)\times SU(\ell)\times U(1)
sector

We now use the general formula for the propagator in the self-dual
Q=k/N
background, eqn. (
C
), to construct the propagators around the instanton in the
\Delta
-expansion. To this order, we need to find expressions for the eigenvalues and eigenvectors of the covariant adjoint Laplacian (
246
).

In this section, we concentrate on adjoint components
\phi_{ij}
lying entirely within the “diagonal,” i.e. the
SU(k)\times SU(\ell)\times U(1)
part of the
SU(N)
-adjoint. These components of the adjoint fields do not couple to the order-
\Delta^{0}
background (
46
), since, being proportional to
\omega
(
34
), it commutes with the fields inside
SU(k)\times SU(\ell)\times U(1)
. Therefore, all
k^{2}
diagonal components (we add the one along the
U(1)
generator) obey the free Laplace equation, eqn. (
246
) with
D_{\mu}=\partial_{\mu}

However, the diagonal fields are subject to the boundary conditions (
54
,
55
). In order to find a basis of free fields obeying these BCS, it is convenient to split the diagonal fields into
SU(k)\times U(1)
components (i.e. ones along the traceless part of the
k\times k
part of the
SU(N)
matrix plus the ones proportional to
\omega
) and
SU(\ell)
components (i.e. in the traceless part of the
\ell\times\ell
part of
SU(N)
.

D.1
The
SU(k)\times U(1)
diagonal components

We first recall that the free laplacian on
{\mathbb{T}}^{4}
with periodic BC, has
2^{4}
real, normalized as in (
248
), eigenfunctions of the Laplacian for every
\omega_{n}^{2}
, i.e. for each choice of “momenta”
n_{1,2,3,4}>0
:

\displaystyle\phi_{(n_{\mu},f_{\mu})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{4\over\sqrt{V}}\prod_{\mu}f_{\mu}(n_{\mu}x_{\mu})~,

(311)

\displaystyle f_{\mu}(n_{\mu}x_{\mu})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\cos{2\pi n_{\mu}x_{\mu}\over L_{\mu}},~\text{or}~\sin{2\pi n_{\mu}x_{\mu}\over L_{\mu}}~.

All these have the same
\omega_{n}^{2}=\sum_{\mu}({2\pi n_{\mu}\over L_{\mu}})^{2}
. That these
\phi_{(n,f_{\mu})}
are correctly normalized follows from recalling that
\int\limits_{0}^{L}dx\cos({2\pi nx\over L})\cos({2\pi mx\over L})={L\over 2}\delta_{nm}
, with an identical expression for the integral with
\cos\rightarrow\sin
, while
\sin
and
\cos
are orthogonal.

A look at the BCS (
54
) shows that
\phi_{B^{\prime}C^{\prime}}
is periodic in all directions but
x_{2}
. On the other hand, the
\phi
-component along the
U(1)
generator
\omega
is periodic in all directions. Thus, the only complication that arises is for the
B^{\prime}\neq C^{\prime}
components of the adjoint
\phi_{B^{\prime}C^{\prime}}
, which are not periodic in the
x_{2}
direction.

D.1.1
The Cartan components of the diagonal
SU(k)\times U(1)
: the propagator of the
{\cal{O}}(\Delta^{0})
nonzero modes

Thus, for each of the diagonal components
\phi_{B^{\prime}B^{\prime}}
we find the following orthonormal basis of
2^{4}
eigenfunctions for each eigenvalue
\omega_{n}^{2}
of the Laplacian:
76
76

76

Below, for brevity we keep all
n_{\mu}>0
, but it is easy to see that our considerations generalize to relaxing this to
\sum\limits_{\mu}n_{\mu}n_{\mu}>0
, i.e. allowing some but not all
n_{\mu}
to vanish, thus including all nonzero
\omega_{n}^{2}
.

\displaystyle\phi_{B^{\prime}B^{\prime}\;(n_{\mu},f_{\mu})}(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{2^{4\over 2}\over\sqrt{V}}\prod\limits_{\mu=1}^{4}f_{\mu}(x_{\mu})~,~\text{for every}~B^{\prime},~2^{4}~\text{eigenfunctions}

\displaystyle\omega_{n}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{\mu=1}^{4}({2\pi n_{\mu}\over L_{\mu}})^{2}~,~n_{1,2,3,4}>0,~~(\text{we rename}~\phi_{B^{\prime}B^{\prime}}\rightarrow\phi_{b},\text{see below}).

(312)

Recall that there are
k
zero eigenvalues of the order-
\Delta^{0}
Laplacian, which lie in the
SU(k)\times U(1)
subspace. These are lifted at subleading order in
\Delta
. The contribution of these eigenvalues and the corresponding eigenfunction to the propagator will be computed separately, see the following section
D.1.2
.

In order to include the
U(1)
mode in the
\omega
-direction (
34
) in a proper manner,
let now
\bm{H}_{(k)}
be the Hermitean
SU(k)
Cartan generators (which we complete into
N\times N
matrices by simply adding appropriate zeros). Let us also define the
U(1)
generator along
\omega
as
\frac{\omega}{2\pi\sqrt{k(N-k)}}
. Then combine these into a new basis of
SU(k)\times U(1)
Cartan generators, obeying the relations given below

\displaystyle\bm{\tilde{H}}\equiv\left(\frac{\omega}{2\pi\sqrt{Nk(N-k)}},\bm{H}_{(k)}\right),~~\mbox{tr}\left[\tilde{H}^{b_{1}}\tilde{H}^{b_{2}}\right]=\delta_{b_{1}b_{2}},~~b_{1},b_{2}=1,2,..,k.

(313)

Using this basis, we can group the
SU(k)\times U(1)
components of the adjoints
\phi
as fields along the
SU(k)
Cartan directions and the
U(1)

\phi_{\omega}
into one set of fields
\tilde{\phi}_{i}
, where
i=1,2,..,k
. In view of the Cartan normalization given above, we simply replace
\phi_{B^{\prime}B^{\prime}}\rightarrow\tilde{\phi}_{i}
to define the
k
eigenmodes of the Laplacian given in (
D.1.1
). Likewise, the fields
\lambda,\bar{\lambda}
have components along the
SU(k)\times U(1)
Cartan generators (
313
) which we denote by
\tilde{\lambda}_{i}
and whose propagators will be read off—after first working out the contribution of
\phi_{i}
to (
C
). Explicitly, we define
the gaugino components along the
SU(k)\times U(1)
Cartan generators (
313
)

\displaystyle\lambda(x)=\sum\limits_{b=1}^{k}\tilde{\lambda}_{b}\tilde{H}^{b}+\text{off diagonal}

(314)

and in what follows, find their propagator from (
C
).

To obtain the propagator
\langle\lambda_{b}\lambda_{b^{\prime}}\rangle
, from (
C
), one has, for every
\omega_{n}^{2}
, to sum over all
2^{4}
different choices of wave functions and their derivatives appearing in (
C
). The
\sum_{n}
in the nonzero-mode contribution to the propagator can thus be written as a sum over the values of
\omega_{n}^{2}
, i.e. a sum over
n_{\mu}>0
, and a sum over the
2^{4}
various choices of
f_{\mu}
for a fixed

\omega_{n}^{2}=(2\pi)^{2}\sum_{\mu}{n_{\mu}^{2}/L_{\mu}^{2}}
.

We start with the sum over
f_{\mu}
appearing in the
22
element of the propagator for a given
\omega_{n}^{2}
:

\displaystyle\sum_{f_{\mu}}\phi_{(n_{\mu},f_{\mu})}(x)\phi_{(n_{\mu},f_{\mu})}(y)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{16\over V}\sum_{\{f\}=\{\cos,\sin\}}\prod_{\lambda}f_{\lambda}(x_{\lambda})f_{\lambda}(y_{\lambda})

(315)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{16\over V}\prod_{\lambda}\left(\sum_{f_{\lambda}=\{\cos(...),\sin(...)\}}f_{\lambda}(x_{\lambda})f_{\lambda}(y_{\lambda})\right)

We now note that for every
\lambda=1,...,4
,

\displaystyle\sum_{f_{\lambda}(...)=\{\cos({2\pi n_{\lambda}...\over L_{\lambda}}),\sin{2\pi n_{\lambda}...\over L_{\lambda}})\}}f_{\lambda}(x_{\lambda})f_{\lambda}(y_{\lambda})={1\over 2}\left(e^{i{2\pi n_{\lambda}\over L_{\lambda}}(x_{\lambda}-y_{\lambda})}+e^{-i{2\pi n_{\lambda}\over L_{\lambda}}(x_{\lambda}-y_{\lambda})}\right),

(316)

meaning that we can, instead of summing over positive values of
n_{\mu}
only, sum over positive and negative values. Thus, the
\bar{\lambda}(x)\bar{\lambda}(y)
-propagator from (
C
) can be equivalently written as:

\displaystyle\langle\bar{\lambda}_{b}^{\dot{\alpha}}(x)\bar{\lambda}_{b^{\prime}\dot{\beta}}(y)\rangle_{unnorm.}=\delta_{bb^{\prime}}{\cal{D}}_{k}^{f}(m)\;{g^{2}\over 2V}\sum\limits_{p_{\mu}\in{2\pi\over L_{\mu}}{\mathbb{Z}}}^{\prime}{\delta_{\dot{\beta}}^{\dot{\alpha}}m\over p_{\mu}^{2}+|m|^{2}}e^{ip_{\mu}(x_{\mu}-y_{\mu})}~,

(317)

where the prime on the sum denotes omission of
p_{\mu}=0
.

Next, consider the sums appearing in the
12
and
21
matrix elements in (
C
) (where for definiteness and to not overcrowd the notation) we took the derivative in the
x_{1}
-direction:

\displaystyle\sum_{f_{\mu}}{\partial_{1}\phi_{(n_{\mu},f_{\mu})}(x)\phi_{(n_{\mu},f_{\mu})}(y)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{16\over V}\sum_{\{f\}=\{\cos,\sin\}}{\partial_{1}f_{1}(x_{1})f_{1}(y_{1})}\prod_{\lambda=2,3,4}f_{\lambda}(x_{\lambda})f_{\lambda}(y_{\lambda})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{1\over V}{i2\pi n_{1}\over L_{1}}(e^{i{2\pi n_{1}\over L_{1}}(x_{1}-y_{1})}-e^{-i{2\pi n_{1}\over L_{1}}(x_{1}-y_{1})})\prod_{\lambda=2,3,4}\left(e^{i{2\pi n_{\lambda}\over L_{\lambda}}(x_{\lambda}-y_{\lambda})}+e^{-i{2\pi n_{\lambda}\over L_{\lambda}}(x_{\lambda}-y_{\lambda})}\right)~.

Here, we used (
316
) in the
\lambda=2,3,4
directions, as well as (giving also the equation for the case when the derivative acts on
y_{1}
)

\displaystyle\sum_{f_{1}(...)=\{\cos({2\pi n_{1}...\over L_{1}}),\sin{2\pi n_{1}...\over L_{1}})\}}\partial_{1}f_{1}(x_{1})f_{1}(y_{1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{1\over 2}{i2\pi n_{1}\over L_{1}}(e^{i{2\pi n_{1}\over L_{1}}(x_{1}-y_{1})}-e^{-i{2\pi n_{1}\over L_{1}}(x_{1}-y_{1})})

\displaystyle\sum_{f_{1}(...)=\{\cos({2\pi n_{1}...\over L_{1}}),\sin{2\pi n_{1}...\over L_{1}})\}}f_{1}(x_{1})\partial_{1}f_{1}(y_{1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-{1\over 2}{i2\pi n_{1}\over L_{1}}(e^{i{2\pi n_{1}\over L_{1}}(x_{1}-y_{1})}-e^{-i{2\pi n_{1}\over L_{1}}(x_{1}-y_{1})})

Clearly this holds with
x_{1}
replaced any other direction and allows us to extend the summation over
n_{\mu}>0
to both positive and negative
n_{\mu}
, giving for the
12
and
21
propagators in (
C
) the expressions:

\displaystyle\langle\lambda_{b\alpha}(x)\bar{\lambda}_{b^{\prime}\;\dot{\beta}}(y)\rangle_{unnorm.}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta_{bb^{\prime}}{\cal{D}}_{k}^{f}(m)\;{g^{2}\over 2V}\sum\limits_{p_{\mu}\in{2\pi\over L_{\mu}}{\mathbb{Z}}}^{\prime}{i\sigma_{\mu\;\alpha\dot{\beta}}p_{\mu}\over p_{\mu}^{2}+|m|^{2}}e^{ip_{\mu}(x_{\mu}-y_{\mu})}~,

\displaystyle\langle\bar{\lambda}^{\dot{\alpha}}_{b}(x)\lambda_{b^{\prime}}^{\beta}(y)\rangle_{unnorm.}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta_{bb^{\prime}}{\cal{D}}_{k}^{f}(m)\;{g^{2}\over 2V}\sum\limits_{p_{\mu}\in{2\pi\over L_{\mu}}{\mathbb{Z}}}^{\prime}{i\bar{\sigma}_{\nu}^{\dot{\alpha}\beta}p_{\nu}\over p_{\mu}^{2}+|m|^{2}}e^{ip_{\mu}(x_{\mu}-y_{\mu})}~,

(320)

Finally, we consider the
11
element,
(\sigma_{\mu}\bar{\sigma}_{\nu})_{\alpha}^{\;\;\beta}\;{D_{\mu}\phi_{n}(x)\;D_{\nu}\phi_{n}(y)\over\omega_{n}^{2}}
, for which, in view of (
C
) we only need to consider
\sum_{\mu}{D_{\mu}\phi_{n}(x)\;D_{\mu}\phi_{n}(y)\over\omega_{n}^{2}}
as well as the antisymmetric combination appearing in the second term of (
C
). We start with the latter

\displaystyle\sum_{f_{\mu}}{\partial_{1}\phi_{(n_{\mu},f_{\mu})}(x)\partial_{2}\phi_{(n_{\mu},f_{\mu})}(y)}-{\partial_{2}\phi_{(n_{\mu},f_{\mu})}(x)\partial_{1}\phi_{(n_{\mu},f_{\mu})}(y)}

(321)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{16\over V}\left(\sum\limits_{f_{1}}(\partial_{1}f_{1}(x_{1})f_{1}(y_{1}))(\sum_{f_{2}}f_{2}(x_{2})\partial_{2}f_{2}(y_{2}))-(\sum_{f_{1}}f_{1}(x_{1})\partial_{1}f_{1}(y_{1}))(\sum_{f_{2}}\partial_{2}f_{2}(x_{2})f_{2}(y_{2}))\right)

\displaystyle~\times\prod_{\lambda=3,4}(\sum_{f_{\lambda}}f_{\lambda}(x_{\lambda})f_{\lambda}(y_{\lambda}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0,

where the vanishing of the second line follows from applying (
D.1.1
). Thus, we now focus on
\sum_{\mu}{D_{\mu}\phi_{n}(x)\;D_{\mu}\phi_{n}(y)\over\omega_{n}^{2}}
, from which we compute one term, taking
\mu=1
and momentarily omitting the
1/\omega_{n}^{2}
factor:

\displaystyle\sum_{f}\partial_{1}\phi_{n_{\mu},f_{\mu}}(x)\;\partial_{1}\phi_{n_{\mu},f_{\mu}}(y)

(322)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{16\over V}\sum_{\{f\}=\{\cos,\sin\}}{\partial_{1}f_{1}(x_{1})\partial_{1}f_{1}(y_{1})}\prod_{\lambda=2,3,4}f_{\lambda}(x_{\lambda})f_{\lambda}(y_{\lambda})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{1\over V}\left({2\pi n_{1}\over L_{1}}\right)^{2}(e^{i{2\pi n_{1}\over L_{1}}(x_{1}-y_{1})}+e^{-i{2\pi n_{1}\over L_{1}}(x_{1}-y_{1})})\prod_{\lambda=2,3,4}\left(e^{i{2\pi n_{\lambda}\over L_{\lambda}}(x_{\lambda}-y_{\lambda})}+e^{-i{2\pi n_{\lambda}\over L_{\lambda}}(x_{\lambda}-y_{\lambda})}\right)~,

where the identity used to go from the second to the third line is easily verified.
Replacing
1\rightarrow\mu
, summing over
\mu
, remembering the zero mode wave functions (
80
) as well as the
1\over\omega_{n}^{2}
factor, we find that
the
11
-propagator in (
C
) is:

\displaystyle\langle\lambda_{b\;\alpha}(x)\lambda_{b^{\prime}}^{\beta}(y)\rangle_{unnorm.}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta_{bb^{\prime}}\;\delta_{\alpha}^{\beta}\;{\cal{D}}_{k}^{f}(m){g^{2}\over 2V}\left({m^{*}\over|m|^{2}}+\sum\limits_{p_{\mu}\in{2\pi\over L_{\mu}}{\mathbb{Z}}}^{\prime}{m^{*}\over p_{\mu}^{2}+|m|^{2}}e^{ip_{\mu}(x_{\mu}-y_{\mu})}\right),

where we also remembered the zero mode wavefunctions (
80
).

To summarize, here we found the propagators along the Cartan generators of the diagonal
SU(k)\times U(1)
. They are given in (
D.1.1
,
D.1.1
,
334
).
We stress
that to these propagators, we have to add the contribution of the lowest eigenvalue of the Laplacian, which is order
\Delta
.

D.1.2
The Cartan components of the diagonal
SU(k)\times U(1)
: the lifting of the
{\cal{O}}(\Delta^{0})
zero modes at order
\Delta

The covariant Laplacian (
246
), in the
{\cal{O}}(\Delta^{0})
background, has
k
zero modes: these are constant modes in the Cartan directions of
SU(k)\times U(1)
.
77
77

77

As our results in the various sections here show, upon decomposing the Laplacian into different
SU(N)
components, the
SU(\ell)
as well as the off-diagonal
k\times\ell
and
\ell\times k
blocks have no zero modes at order
\Delta^{0}
.
Similar to the definition of the Cartan components of the gaugino (
314
), we define these unperturbed hermitean zero modes,
\phi^{(0)}
, using the
\bm{\tilde{H}}
basis of (
313
):

\displaystyle\phi^{(0)b}={1\over\sqrt{V}}\;\tilde{H}^{b},~b=1,...k,~\text{with}~\int\limits_{{\mathbb{T}}^{4}}\mathrm{tr}\,\phi^{(0)b}\phi^{(0)a}=\delta^{ab}.

(324)

We also recall that
b=1
corresponds to the
U(1)
-generator and
b=2,...,k
—to the
SU(k)
Cartan generators from (
313
) and that the basis is
N\times N
dimensional (with the
SU(k)
Cartan generators extended by appropriately adding zeros).

At next order in
\Delta
, the zero modes of the Laplacian—and, by eqns. (
C
) and (
C
), of the dotted fermions—are lifted. Here, we calculate the shift of the lowest eigenvalues of Laplacian away from zero to leading nontrivial order in
\Delta
. Such an estimate was carried out in
Anber:2022qsz
in the
SU(2)
case, and we generalize it here for
SU(N)
and arbitrary
k
.

We use first-order perturbation theory to determine the shift of the lowest eigenvalue of the operator
-D_{\mu}D_{\mu}
away from zero.
To this order, we need to find the eigenvalues of the
k\times k
matrix

\displaystyle E^{ba}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\int\limits_{{\mathbb{T}}^{4}}\mathrm{tr}\,\phi^{(0)b}(-D_{\mu}D_{\mu})_{pert.}\phi^{(0)a}=\int\limits_{{\mathbb{T}}^{4}}\mathrm{tr}\,(D_{\mu})_{pert.}\phi^{(0)b}(D_{\mu})_{pert.}\phi^{(0)a}

(325)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int\limits_{{\mathbb{T}}^{4}}\mathrm{tr}\,[A_{\mu}^{pert.},\phi^{(0)b}][A_{\mu}^{pert.},\phi^{(0)a}]

We used the definition of the covariant derivative, see eqn. (
13
). The leading-order
\Delta
-expansion of the background (
2.3
) shows that the background
A_{\mu}
is perturbed by, to order
\sqrt{\Delta}
, by:

\displaystyle A_{\mu}^{pert.}=\sqrt{\Delta}\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}0&w_{\mu}\\
w_{\mu}^{\dagger}&0\end{array}\right]+{\Delta}\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}{\cal{S}}^{(k)}_{\mu}&0\\
0&{\cal{S}}^{(\ell)}_{\mu}\end{array}\right]+{\cal{O}}(\Delta^{3\over 2})\,,

using the
k
-
\ell
block decomposition of
SU(N)
as in (
2.3
) (the leading order perturbation
w_{\mu}
is in the
k\times\ell
and
\ell\times k
components of the
N\times N
matrix). Then, we find, to order
\Delta
:

\displaystyle E^{11}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{2N\Delta\over k(N-k)}\sum\limits_{C^{\prime}=1}^{k}\sum\limits_{D=1}^{\ell}{1\over V}\int\limits_{{\mathbb{T}}^{4}}(w_{\mu}^{\dagger})_{DC^{\prime}}(w_{\mu})_{C^{\prime}D}

\displaystyle E^{1b}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{2\Delta\over k(N-k)}\sum\limits_{C^{\prime}=1}^{k}(\tilde{H}^{b})_{C^{\prime}C^{\prime}}\sum\limits_{D=1}^{\ell}{1\over V}\int\limits_{{\mathbb{T}}^{4}}(w_{\mu})_{C^{\prime}D}(w_{\mu}^{\dagger})_{DC^{\prime}},~\text{for}~b=2,...,k,

(331)

\displaystyle E^{ab}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{2}\Delta\sum\limits_{C^{\prime}=1}^{k}(\tilde{H}^{a}\tilde{H}^{b})_{C^{\prime}C^{\prime}}\sum\limits_{D=1}^{\ell}{1\over V}\int\limits_{{\mathbb{T}}^{4}}(w_{\mu})_{C^{\prime}D}(w_{\mu}^{\dagger})_{DC^{\prime}},~\text{for}~a,b=2,...,k~.

To proceed, we have to evaluate the integral entering all energy shifts above. Using the formulae
78
78

78

For the reader who wants to reproduce (
332
), we offer a guide to the relevant equations in
Anber:2023sjn
: the functions
(w_{\mu})_{C^{\prime}D}
are defined in eqns. (4.20, 4.21) there, while

is in (3.21). For

{\cal{C}}_{4}^{A}=0
, while

D.1.2
) is then computed yielding the quoted result.
from
Anber:2023sjn
, we find,
\forall C^{\prime}=1,\ldots k
:

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{D=1}^{\ell}{1\over V}\int\limits_{{\mathbb{T}}^{4}}(w_{\mu})_{C^{\prime}D}(w_{\mu}^{\dagger})_{DC^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{2\pi\over kN}{1\over\sqrt{V}}~\rightarrow{2\pi\over kN}{1\over L^{2}},

(332)

where we replaced
\sqrt{V}
by
L^{2}
for brevity. This then gives for (
D.1.2
) the diagonal result

\displaystyle E^{11}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{4\pi\over k(N-k)}{\Delta\over L^{2}}~,

\displaystyle E^{1b}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0~,

(333)

\displaystyle E^{ab}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{4\pi\over kN}{\Delta\over L^{2}}\;\mathrm{tr}\,\tilde{H}^{a}\tilde{H}^{b}=\delta^{ab}{4\pi\over kN}{\Delta\over L^{2}},\;\text{for}\;a,b=2,...,k~.

Because of the diagonality of the above matrix elements, there is no need to change the unperturbed basis of eigenvectors, and we take the leading-order wave functions to be simply the ones in (
324
),
\phi^{(0)b}
.
From (
C
), we then find that
the contribution of the order-
\Delta
eigenvalues affects the
\bar{\lambda}-\bar{\lambda}
propagator. Adding to
(
334
) the contribution of the eigenvalues (
D.1.2
), we find

\displaystyle\langle\bar{\lambda}_{b}^{\dot{\alpha}}(x)\bar{\lambda}_{b^{\prime}\dot{\beta}}(y)\rangle_{unnorm.}=\delta_{bb^{\prime}}{\cal{D}}_{k}^{f}(m)\;{g^{2}\over 2V}\sum\limits_{p_{\mu}\in{2\pi\over L_{\mu}}{\mathbb{Z}}}^{\prime}{\delta_{\dot{\beta}}^{\dot{\alpha}}m\over p_{\mu}^{2}+|m|^{2}}e^{ip_{\mu}(x_{\mu}-y_{\mu})}~

(334)

\displaystyle+\delta_{bb^{\prime}}\delta_{b1}{\cal{D}}_{k}^{f}(m)\;{g^{2}\over 2V}\delta^{\dot{\alpha}}_{\dot{\beta}}{m\over{4\pi\over k(N-k)}{\Delta\over L^{2}}+|m|^{2}}~+\delta_{bb^{\prime}}\sum\limits_{a=2}^{k}\delta_{ba}{\cal{D}}_{k}^{f}(m)\;{g^{2}\over 2V}\delta^{\dot{\alpha}}_{\dot{\beta}}{m\over{4\pi\over kN}{\Delta\over L^{2}}+|m|^{2}}.

We next recall that the SYM result is reproduced by taking
m\rightarrow 0
while keeping
\Delta
strictly positive, hence, parametrically
\Delta\gg(mL)^{2}
. Thus, including the coefficient, in what follows we study the small soft mass limit where
|m|L
obeys

\displaystyle{|m|L\over\sqrt{c\Delta}}\ll 1~,~c={4\pi\over kN}~.

(335)

In this limit, further assuming
79
79

79

For brevity only: this assumption allows us to write the terms on the last line in (
334
) as one.
that
k\ll N
, we rewrite the propagator (
334
) in the simpler form

\displaystyle\langle\bar{\lambda}_{b}^{\dot{\alpha}}(x)\bar{\lambda}_{b^{\prime}\dot{\beta}}(y)\rangle_{unnorm.}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta_{bb^{\prime}}{\cal{D}}_{k}^{f}(m)\;{g^{2}\over 2V}\sum\limits_{p_{\mu}\in{2\pi\over L_{\mu}}{\mathbb{Z}}}^{\prime}{\delta_{\dot{\beta}}^{\dot{\alpha}}m\over p_{\mu}^{2}+|m|^{2}}e^{ip_{\mu}(x_{\mu}-y_{\mu})}~

(336)

\displaystyle+\delta_{bb^{\prime}}{\cal{D}}_{k}^{f}(m)\;{g^{2}\over 2V}\;\delta^{\dot{\alpha}}_{\dot{\beta}}\;{mL^{2}\over c\Delta}(1-{|m|^{2}L^{2}\over c\Delta}+\ldots).

Let us now make some comments on the limit (
335
) and the expressions for the propagators and eigenvalues in the
Q={k/N}
background that we use throughout this paper:

1.

From now on, we shall ignore terms suppressed in the limit (
335
) and keep only the leading term in the propagator (
336
), proportional to
\sim{mL^{2}\over c\Delta}
.

2.

We also note that all Laplacian eigenvalues receive order-
\Delta
corrections. This includes the
p_{\mu}^{2}\neq 0
eigenvalues of the free Laplacian whose contributions to the propagator are on the first line in (
336
). These corrections are in principle computable via perturbation theory, but this is an arduous task shall not attempt here. Thus, in the limit (
335
), we ignore the additive
|m|^{2}
terms in the denominator of the first line in (
336
) and replace the propagator (
336
) by:

\displaystyle\langle\bar{\lambda}_{b}^{\dot{\alpha}}(x)\bar{\lambda}_{b^{\prime}\dot{\beta}}(y)\rangle_{unnorm.}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta_{bb^{\prime}}{\cal{D}}_{k}^{f}(m)\;{g^{2}\over 2V}\;\delta_{\dot{\beta}}^{\dot{\alpha}}\;\left({mL^{2}\over c\Delta}+\sum\limits_{p_{\mu}\in{2\pi\over L_{\mu}}{\mathbb{Z}}}^{\prime}{m\;e^{ip_{\mu}(x_{\mu}-y_{\mu})}\over p_{\mu}^{2}}\right),

recalling that the sum excludes
p_{\mu}^{2}=0
.

(a)

We also note that the sum in the second term in the propagator (
2
), while divergent and in need of regularization (we discuss this later), scales as
mL^{2}
, and is thus parametrically suppressed compared to the first term, due to the fact that
\Delta\ll 1
.

(b)

We stress that the comment here applies to all other propagators in the
Q={k/N}
background computed in the
\Delta
expansion: the
|m|^{2}
contributions in the denominators are to be ignored in the
(|m|L)^{2}\ll\Delta
limit.

3.

Finally, we note that, in contrast with the
22
element (
336
), the
11
,
12
, and
21
elements of the propagator (
C
) do not receive a similar
{1\over\Delta}
contribution, since the leading-order unperturbed eigenvectors (
324
) obey
\partial_{\mu}\phi^{(0)b}=0
.

There are, however, other contributions to the
11,12
and
21
elements of the propagator (
C
) from the order-
\Delta^{0}
zero eigenvalues of the Laplacian (lifted at order
\Delta
), but which do not scale as
1\over\Delta
and are in principle computable.
To see this, we note that the background entering the covariant derivatives in the
11
,
12
, and
21
elements of (
C
) is shifted at order
\sqrt{\Delta}
. The eigenvectors (
324
) are also shifted at order-
\Delta
, in a manner computable in perturbation theory. Both these shifts conspire to cancel the
1\over\Delta
contribution from
\omega_{n}^{2}=0+{\cal{O}}(\Delta)
and lead to order-
1
contributions to the propagators. In the spirit of keeping only the leading
|m|^{2}L^{2}\over c\Delta
terms in all our expansions, we shall not need to compute these corrections here.

D.1.3
The propagator of the non-Cartan components of the diagonal
SU(k)\times U(1)

We now continue with finding the free Laplacian eigenfunctions with
B^{\prime}\neq C^{\prime}
, obeying the BCS (
54
). For every choice of an above-the-diagonal element
B^{\prime},C^{\prime}
(such that
B^{\prime}>C^{\prime}
; recall that there are
k(k-1)/2
such choices) there are Hermitean eigenfunctions of the Laplacian which involve both the
\phi_{B^{\prime}C^{\prime}}
and
\phi_{C^{\prime}B^{\prime}}
components, with all other components vanishing.
80
80

80

As opposed to the Cartan sector, in the off-diagonal part of the
SU(k)
matrix, hermiticity can be satisfied with complex eigenfunctions, explicitly given in (
338
).
The eigenfunctions are labeled by a set of integers
n_{\mu}
as well as a choice of
B^{\prime}>C^{\prime}
. Thus, the eigenfunction of the Laplacian
\phi_{(n_{\mu},B^{\prime}>C^{\prime})}
is a
k\times k
hermitean matrix with only two nonzero off-diagonal entries, which are complex conjugates to each other. There are two such linearly independent Hermitean matrices for every choice of
B^{\prime}>C^{\prime}
, corresponding to the fact that the Hermitean matrices (schematically, showing only the two nonzero entries inside the
k\times k
matrix)

\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&f\cr f^{*}&0\end{array}\right)~\text{and}\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&if\cr-if^{*}&0\end{array}\right)

are linearly independent under addition with real coefficients (a restriction needed in order to preserve the Hermiticity of the sum of two arbitrary hermitean matrices).

In a somewhat baroque but explicit notation, we use
(\phi_{(n_{\mu},B^{\prime}>C^{\prime},\alpha)})_{D^{\prime}E^{\prime}}
to label the
D^{\prime}E^{\prime}
element of the
k\times k
matrix representing the Hermitean eigenfunction of the free Laplacian:

\displaystyle(\phi_{(n_{\mu},B^{\prime}>C^{\prime},\alpha)})_{D^{\prime}E^{\prime}}

(338)

\displaystyle={1\over\sqrt{2}}{\alpha\over\sqrt{V}}e^{i2\pi(n_{\mu}+\delta_{\mu 2}{B^{\prime}-C^{\prime}\over k}){x_{\mu}\over L_{\mu}}}\delta_{B^{\prime}D^{\prime}}\delta_{C^{\prime}E^{\prime}}+{1\over\sqrt{2}}{\alpha^{*}\over\sqrt{V}}e^{-i2\pi(n_{\mu}+\delta_{\mu 2}{B^{\prime}-C^{\prime}\over k}){x_{\mu}\over L_{\mu}}}\delta_{B^{\prime}E^{\prime}}\delta_{D^{\prime}C^{\prime}},

\displaystyle\text{with eigenvalue}~\omega_{n}^{2}=\sum\limits_{\mu=1}^{4}\left(2\pi\;{n_{\mu}+\delta_{\mu 2}{B^{\prime}-C^{\prime}\over k}\over L_{\mu}}\right)^{2}~,~~n_{\mu}\in{\mathbb{Z}},~\alpha=\{1,i\}~,

where the normalization factor is worked out in (
D.1.3
) below (and solely for brevity we did not indicate the
B^{\prime}C^{\prime}
-dependence when denoting the eigenvalue by
\omega_{n}^{2}
).
Clearly the boundary conditions (
54
) are obeyed. Hermiticity is also manifest, as
(\phi_{(n_{\mu},B^{\prime}>C^{\prime},\alpha)})_{D^{\prime}E^{\prime}}^{*}=(\phi_{(n_{\mu},B^{\prime}>C^{\prime},\alpha)})_{E^{\prime}D^{\prime}}
. The fact that the normalization condition (
248
) holds follows by explicit calculation from (
338
). Mindful about the
B^{\prime}>C^{\prime}
and
E^{\prime}>F^{\prime}
condition, we obtain for the inner product:

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{4}}\mathrm{tr}\,\phi_{(n_{\mu},B^{\prime}>C^{\prime},\alpha)}\phi_{(m_{\mu},E^{\prime}>F^{\prime},\beta)}=\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{4}}\sum\limits_{D^{\prime},G^{\prime}=1}^{k}(\phi_{(n_{\mu},B^{\prime}>C^{\prime},\alpha})_{D^{\prime}G^{\prime}}(x)(\phi_{(m_{\mu},E^{\prime}>F^{\prime},\beta)})_{G^{\prime}D^{\prime}}(x)

\displaystyle={1\over V}\delta_{B^{\prime}E^{\prime}}\delta_{C^{\prime}F^{\prime}}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{4}}d^{4}x({\alpha^{*}\beta\over 2}e^{-\sum\limits_{\mu=1}^{4}i2\pi(n_{\mu}-m_{\mu}){x_{\mu}\over L_{\mu}}}+{\alpha\beta^{*}\over 2}e^{\sum\limits_{\mu=1}^{4}i2\pi(n_{\mu}-m_{\mu}){x_{\mu}\over L_{\mu}}})

\displaystyle=\delta_{B^{\prime}E^{\prime}}\delta_{C^{\prime}F^{\prime}}\prod_{\mu}\delta_{n_{\mu}m_{\mu}}~{\alpha\beta^{*}+\alpha^{*}\beta\over 2}=\delta_{B^{\prime}E^{\prime}}\delta_{C^{\prime}F^{\prime}}\delta_{\alpha\beta}\prod_{\mu}\delta_{n_{\mu}m_{\mu}},

(339)

remembering that
\alpha
and
\beta
are either
1
or
i
and using
\delta_{\alpha\beta}=1
if
\alpha=\beta
and zero otherwise. In eqn. (
D.1.3
), we have thus showed that satisfying (
248
) requires the overall
{1\over\sqrt{2}}
factor in (
338
), and that the different eigenfunctions are indeed orthogonal.

Before we continue, we also note that since each eigenfunction (
338
) only involves the
\phi_{B^{\prime}C^{\prime}}
and
\phi_{C^{\prime}B^{\prime}}
components with some given
B^{\prime}\neq C^{\prime}
, the only nonzero propagators are of the form

\langle\lambda_{B^{\prime}C^{\prime}}(x)\lambda_{C^{\prime}B^{\prime}}(y)\rangle
, etc., as follows from (
C
) and the outer product definition (
4
), and as we now show in detail.
We begin by evaluating the contribution of the eigenfunctions with
B^{\prime}>C^{\prime}
(summed over
n_{\mu}
,
\alpha=1,i
, and over
B^{\prime}>C^{\prime}
, i.e. over all eigenvalues of the Laplacian) to the
22
element of (
C
), remembering that it is understood as an outer product, i.e. carries two sets of adjoint indices as in (
4
). On one hand, this matrix element gives the unnormalized propagator

\displaystyle\langle\bar{\lambda}^{\dot{\alpha}}_{D^{\prime}E^{\prime}}(x)\bar{\lambda}_{\dot{\beta}\;F^{\prime}G^{\prime}}(y)\rangle~,

(340)

where we recall that here
D^{\prime}\neq E^{\prime}
and
F^{\prime}\neq G^{\prime}
, because we are computing the off-diagonal
SU(k)
elements in this section.
By (
C
) the
D^{\prime}\neq E^{\prime}
,
F^{\prime}\neq G^{\prime}
matrix element (
340
) is equal to the sum over all eigenfunctions (
338
), i.e. over all
n_{\mu}
and
B^{\prime}>C^{\prime}
:

\displaystyle{g^{2}\over 2}{\cal{D}}_{k}^{f}(m)\;\sum\limits_{{n_{\mu},B^{\prime}>C^{\prime},\alpha}}{m\delta^{\dot{\alpha}}_{\dot{\beta}}\over|m|^{2}+\omega_{n}^{2}}\;(\phi_{(n_{\mu},B^{\prime}>C^{\prime},\alpha)})_{D^{\prime}E^{\prime}}(x)(\phi_{(n_{\mu},B^{\prime}>C^{\prime},\alpha)})_{F^{\prime}G^{\prime}}(y)

\displaystyle={g^{2}\over 4V}{\cal{D}}_{k}^{f}(m)\sum\limits_{p_{\mu}={2\pi n_{\mu}\over L_{\mu}},B^{\prime}>C^{\prime}}{m\delta^{\dot{\alpha}}_{\dot{\beta}}\over|m|^{2}+(p_{\mu}+\delta_{\mu 2}{2\pi\over L_{2}}{B^{\prime}-C^{\prime}\over k})^{2}}

(341)

\displaystyle\times\left(e^{i(x_{\mu}+y_{\mu})(p_{\mu}+\delta_{\mu,2}{2\pi(B^{\prime}-C^{\prime})\over kL_{2}})}\delta_{B^{\prime}D^{\prime}}\delta_{C^{\prime}E^{\prime}}\delta_{B^{\prime}F^{\prime}}\delta_{C^{\prime}G^{\prime}}(\sum_{\alpha}\alpha^{2})\right.

\displaystyle\left.~~+e^{i(x_{\mu}-y_{\mu})(p_{\mu}+\delta_{\mu,2}{2\pi(B^{\prime}-C^{\prime})\over kL_{2}})}\delta_{B^{\prime}D^{\prime}}\delta_{C^{\prime}E^{\prime}}\delta_{B^{\prime}G^{\prime}}\delta_{C^{\prime}F^{\prime}}(\sum_{\alpha}|\alpha|^{2})\right.

\displaystyle\left.~~+e^{-i(x_{\mu}-y_{\mu})(p_{\mu}+\delta_{\mu,2}{2\pi(B^{\prime}-C^{\prime})\over kL_{2}})}\delta_{B^{\prime}E^{\prime}}\delta_{C^{\prime}D^{\prime}}\delta_{B^{\prime}F^{\prime}}\delta_{C^{\prime}G^{\prime}}(\sum_{\alpha}|\alpha|^{2})\right.

\displaystyle\left.~~+e^{-i(x_{\mu}+y_{\mu})(p_{\mu}+\delta_{\mu,2}{2\pi(B^{\prime}-C^{\prime})\over kL_{2}})}\delta_{B^{\prime}E^{\prime}}\delta_{C^{\prime}D^{\prime}}\delta_{B^{\prime}G^{\prime}}\delta_{C^{\prime}F^{\prime}}(\sum_{\alpha}(\alpha^{*})^{2}\right)

Recalling that
\alpha=1,i
, the terms with summing
\alpha^{2}
or
(\alpha^{*})^{2}
drop out, while the others give a factor of
2
. The Kronecker symbols remove the sum over group indices, but we have to be conscious about the
B^{\prime}>C^{\prime}
condition. Proceeding carefully, we rewrite (
D.1.3
) as:

\displaystyle{g^{2}\over 2V}{\cal{D}}_{k}^{f}(m)\times

(342)

\displaystyle\left[\sum\limits_{p_{\mu}={2\pi n_{\mu}\over L_{\mu}}}{m\delta^{\dot{\alpha}}_{\dot{\beta}}\over|m|^{2}+(p_{\mu}+\delta_{\mu 2}{2\pi\over L_{2}}{D^{\prime}-E^{\prime}\over k})^{2}}\left(e^{ix_{\mu}(p_{\mu}+\delta_{\mu,2}{2\pi(D^{\prime}-E^{\prime})\over kL_{2}})-iy_{\mu}(p_{\mu}-\delta_{\mu,2}{2\pi(F^{\prime}-G^{\prime})\over kL_{2}})}\delta_{D^{\prime}G^{\prime}}\delta_{E^{\prime}F^{\prime}}\theta_{D^{\prime}E^{\prime}}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\sum\limits_{p_{\mu}={2\pi n_{\mu}\over L_{\mu}}}{m\delta^{\dot{\alpha}}_{\dot{\beta}}\over|m|^{2}+(p_{\mu}-\delta_{\mu 2}{2\pi\over L_{2}}{D^{\prime}-E^{\prime}\over k})^{2}}\left(e^{-ix_{\mu}(p_{\mu}-\delta_{\mu,2}{2\pi(D^{\prime}-E^{\prime})\over kL_{2}})+iy_{\mu}(p_{\mu}+\delta_{\mu,2}{2\pi(F^{\prime}-G^{\prime})\over kL_{2}})}\delta_{D^{\prime}G^{\prime}}\delta_{E^{\prime}F^{\prime}}\theta_{E^{\prime}D^{\prime}}\right)\right],

where we defined the “theta-function”
\theta_{E^{\prime}D^{\prime}}=1
if
E^{\prime}>D^{\prime}
and
\theta_{E^{\prime}D^{\prime}}=0
if
E^{\prime}<D^{\prime}
.
We now note that the two lines have the same Kronecker symbols but opposite theta-functions). We can combine these two lines together, allowing us to drop the theta-function, upon changing the sign of
p_{\mu}
in the second line, giving the following expression for the non-Cartan components of the
\bar{\lambda}\bar{\lambda}
propagator

\displaystyle\langle\bar{\lambda}^{\dot{\alpha}}_{D^{\prime}E^{\prime}}(x)\bar{\lambda}_{\dot{\beta}\;F^{\prime}G^{\prime}}(y)\rangle~

\displaystyle={g^{2}\over 2V}{\cal{D}}_{k}^{f}(m)

(344)

\displaystyle\times\sum\limits_{p_{\mu}={2\pi n_{\mu}\over L_{\mu}}}{m\delta^{\dot{\alpha}}_{\dot{\beta}}\over|m|^{2}+(p_{\mu}+\delta_{\mu 2}{2\pi\over L_{2}}{D^{\prime}-E^{\prime}\over k})^{2}}e^{ix_{\mu}(p_{\mu}+\delta_{\mu,2}{2\pi(D^{\prime}-E^{\prime})\over kL_{2}})-iy_{\mu}(p_{\mu}-\delta_{\mu,2}{2\pi(F^{\prime}-G^{\prime})\over kL_{2}})}\delta_{D^{\prime}G^{\prime}}\delta_{E^{\prime}F^{\prime}}~.

After this exercise, finding the
11
element of (
C
) becomes straightforward. It is easily seen that only the symmetric part of
\sigma_{\mu}\bar{\sigma}_{\nu}
survives (recall (
C
)) with the derivatives canceling the
\omega_{n}^{2}
factor in the denominator and leading to

\displaystyle\langle\lambda_{\alpha\;D^{\prime}E^{\prime}}(x)\lambda^{\beta}_{F^{\prime}G^{\prime}}(y)\rangle

\displaystyle={g^{2}\over 2V}{\cal{D}}_{k}^{f}(m)

(345)

\displaystyle\times\sum\limits_{p_{\mu}={2\pi n_{\mu}\over L_{\mu}}}{m^{*}\delta_{\alpha}^{\beta}\over|m|^{2}+(p_{\mu}+\delta_{\mu 2}{2\pi\over L_{2}}{D^{\prime}-E^{\prime}\over k})^{2}}e^{ix_{\mu}(p_{\mu}+\delta_{\mu,2}{2\pi(D^{\prime}-E^{\prime})\over kL_{2}})-iy_{\mu}(p_{\mu}-\delta_{\mu,2}{2\pi(F^{\prime}-G^{\prime})\over kL_{2}})}\delta_{D^{\prime}G^{\prime}}\delta_{E^{\prime}F^{\prime}}~.

D.2
The propagator of the

Finally, we turn to finding the propagators (

\displaystyle\phi(x+L_{3}\hat{e}_{3})=P_{\ell}\phi(x)P_{\ell}^{-1}\,,\quad\phi(x+L_{4}\hat{e}_{4})=Q_{\ell}\phi(x)Q_{\ell}^{-1}\,,

(346)

where

and

Gonzalez-Arroyo:1982hyq

\displaystyle\phi(x)=\sum_{\bm{p}=(p_{3},p_{4})\neq(0,0)}\phi_{\bm{p}}(x)J_{\bf p}\,,~\phi\in su(\ell),

(347)

where
p_{3},p_{4}
each run from

to
\ell-1
(i.e.
\bm{p}=(p_{3},p_{4})\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\ell}^{2}
), with
\bm{p}\neq\bm{0}
, and

is given by
81
81

81

The phase factors in the definition of

\displaystyle J_{\bm{p}}=e^{-i\frac{\pi p_{3}p_{4}}{\ell}}Q_{\ell}^{-p_{3}}P_{\ell}^{p_{4}}\,,~J_{\bm{p}}^{\dagger}=e^{-i{\pi p_{3}p_{4}\over\ell}}Q_{\ell}^{p_{3}}P_{\ell}^{-p_{4}}\equiv J_{-\bm{p}},

(348)

satisfying the normalization condition

\displaystyle\mbox{tr}\left[J_{\bm{p}}J_{\bm{p}^{\prime}}\right]=\mbox{tr}\left[\bm{1}_{\ell\times\ell}\right]\delta_{\bm{p},-\bm{p}^{\prime}}=\ell\delta_{\bm{p},-\bm{p}^{\prime}}\,(\text{or}~\mbox{tr}\left[J_{\bm{p}}J^{\dagger}_{\bm{p}^{\prime}}\right]=\ell\delta_{\bm{p},\bm{p}^{\prime}}).

(349)

\displaystyle\phi_{\bm{p}}(x+L_{3}\hat{e}_{3})

\displaystyle\phi_{\bm{p}}(x+L_{1}\hat{e}_{1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\phi_{\bm{p}}(x)\,,\qquad\qquad\;\phi_{\bm{p}}(x+L_{2}\hat{e}_{2})=\phi_{\bm{p}}(x)~.

For the purpose of counting independent solutions, imposing hermiticity, and figuring out the normalization, we stress that in what follows we keep the range of

Next, we also note that the eigenspace of the Laplacian with eigenvalue

E.1
Boundary conditions, eigenvectors of the covariant Laplacian, and norm

To continue, we note that there is a subtlety, related to the BCS (
) for the case

2

The Hermitean eigenvectors

\displaystyle\phi_{n}^{\alpha}\rightarrow\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&\alpha\Phi_{n}\cr\alpha^{*}\Phi_{n}^{\dagger}&0\end{array}\right)~,~\alpha=1~\rm{or}~i.

We write the BCS for
\phi_{n}^{\alpha}
in the general form

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&\alpha\Phi_{n}\cr\alpha^{*}\Phi^{\dagger}&0\end{array}\right)(x+\hat{e}_{\mu}L_{\mu})=\Omega_{\mu}(x)\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&\alpha\Phi_{n}\cr\alpha^{*}\Phi_{n}^{\dagger}&0\end{array}\right)(x)\;\Omega_{\mu}^{\dagger}(x).

Since every element of
\Phi

matrix eigenvector

\displaystyle D^{2}\Phi_{n}=-\omega^{2}_{n}\Phi_{\;n}~.

(385)

The normalization condition (

\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&\alpha\Phi_{n}\cr\alpha^{*}\Phi_{n}^{\dagger}&0\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle\int\limits_{{\mathbb{T}}^{4}}\mathrm{tr}\,_{N\times N}\;\phi_{n}^{\alpha}\phi_{m}^{\beta}=\delta_{\alpha,\beta}\delta_{nm}\rightarrow\int\limits_{{\mathbb{T}}^{4}}\mathrm{tr}\,_{k\times k}(\alpha\beta^{*}\Phi_{n}\cdot\Phi^{\dagger}_{m}+\beta\alpha^{*}\Phi_{m}\cdot\Phi^{\dagger}_{n})=\delta_{\alpha,\beta}\delta_{nm}~.

To recap, the BCS (

To obtain the last line, we used the sum over
m
to extend the
x_{1}
integral over the entire real line and the combined sums over
p
and
C
to extend the
x_{3}
integral to the real line.

The net result is that we have shown that the eigenvalue problem (
376
), subject to the BCS (
), reduces to the standard quantum mechanics problem of two SHOs in the (
x_{1},x_{3}
)-plane, normalizable on the
x_{1},x_{3}
plane, as per (
E.3
). The “Shrödinger equation” is given by eqn. (
E.2
), where the oscillators have frequencies (
403
,
404
). The normalizable wave functions give rise to the eigenvalues
n
labelled by
\ell_{(1)},\ell_{(3)}
which determine the eigenvalue of the Laplacian:

\displaystyle\omega_{n}^{2}\rightarrow\omega^{2}_{\ell_{(1)},\ell_{(3)}}={4\pi\over L_{1}L_{2}}(\ell_{(1)}+\ell_{(3)}+1),~\ell_{(1)},\ell_{(3)}=0,1,2,....

(406)

The eigenfunctions of (
E.2
) are given in terms of the standard unit-normalized eigenfunctions of the one-dimensional Harmonic oscillator,
h_{\ell}(x)
, with eigenvalue
\Omega(\ell+{1\over 2})
,
\ell=0,1,2,...
, explicitly given by:

\displaystyle\Phi_{C^{\prime}n}\rightarrow\Phi_{C^{\prime},\ell_{(1)},\ell_{(3)}}(x_{1},x_{3})=\tilde{c}\;h_{\ell_{(1)}}(x_{1})\;h_{\ell_{(3)}}(x_{3}),

(407)

\displaystyle h_{\ell}(x)={1\over\sqrt{2^{\ell}\ell!}}\left({\Omega\over\pi}\right)^{1\over 4}e^{-{\Omega x^{2}\over 2}}H_{\ell}(\sqrt{\Omega}x),\;\text{and}\;\int\limits_{-\infty}^{\infty}dxh_{\ell}(x)h_{\ell^{\prime}}(x)=\delta_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}~,

where
H_{\ell}(x)
is the
\ell
-th Hermite polynomial.
The normalization
\tilde{c}
must be chosen so that the norm (
E.3
) equals unity,
91
91

91

It is clear that eigenvectors with the same
\ell_{(1)},\ell_{(3)}
but with
\alpha\neq\beta
are orthogonal.
namely:

\displaystyle\tilde{c}={1\over\sqrt{2L_{2}L_{4}}}.

(408)

To summarize, our final expression for the
C
-th component of the eigenvector (
E.1
), the
k\times\ell
matrix
\Phi_{C^{\prime}\ell_{(1)},\ell_{(3)}}
with nonzero
C^{\prime}
-th row, labelled by
(C^{\prime},\ell_{(1)},\ell_{(3)})
is, substituting (
407
) and the normalization (
408
) into (
401
)
92
92

92

Here, we added the (harmless) extra phase factor
e^{i{1+k\over 2k}\pi(1-k)}
, to restore agreement with the similar equations for Dirac zero modes in
Anber:2023sjn
.

\displaystyle\Phi_{C^{\prime}C\;\ell_{(1)},\ell_{(3)}}(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~{1\over\sqrt{2L_{2}L_{4}}}\sum\limits_{m,p\in{\mathbb{Z}}}e^{i{2\pi x_{2}\over L_{2}}(m+{2C^{\prime}-1-k\over 2k})+i{2\pi x_{4}\over L_{4}}(p-{2C-1-\ell\over 2\ell})-i(m+{C^{\prime}\over k}-{1+k\over 2k})\pi(1-k)+i\pi{1-\ell\over\ell}(C-1-p\ell)}

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~\times\;h_{\ell_{(1)}}(x_{1}-L_{1}(m+{C^{\prime}\over k}-{1+k\over 2k}))\;h_{\ell_{(3)}}(x_{3}+L_{3}(C-p\ell-{1+\ell\over 2}))~,

where
h_{\ell}(x)
, as per (

) is the
\ell
-th unit normalized eigenstate of the SHO, and the normalization is such that the norm (

) equals unity. Explicitly, the norm integral (

) is

2\int\limits_{{\mathbb{T}}^{4}}d^{4}x\sum\limits_{C=1}^{\ell}\Phi_{C^{\prime}C\;\ell_{(1)},\ell_{(3)}}(x)\Phi^{*}_{C^{\prime}C\;\ell_{(1)},\ell_{(3)}}(x)=1.

One extra step, useful to obtain expressions similar to our other propagators, is to define a dimensionless
\varphi_{C^{\prime}C\;\ell_{(1)},\ell_{(3)}}
as follows:

\displaystyle\Phi_{C^{\prime}C\;\ell_{(1)},\ell_{(3)}}(x)={1\over\sqrt{2V}}\;\varphi_{C^{\prime}C\;\ell_{(1)},\ell_{(3)}}(x)~,

(410)

where

\displaystyle\int\limits_{{\mathbb{T}}^{4}}d^{4}x\sum\limits_{C=1}^{\ell}\varphi_{C^{\prime}C\;\ell_{(1)},\ell_{(3)}}(x)\varphi^{*}_{C^{\prime}C\;\ell_{(1)},\ell_{(3)}}(x)=V.

(411)

At the end, we also include the instanton moduli from (
48
) (these were previously omitted when writing (
375

\displaystyle\hat{\phi}_{\mu}^{C^{\prime}}={2\pi\over L_{\mu}}\bm{a}_{\mu}\cdot\bm{\nu}_{C^{\prime}}-{2\pi Nz_{\mu}\over L_{\mu}}~,

(412)

where
\bm{\nu}_{C^{\prime}}
are the
k
weights of the fundamental representation of
SU(k)
.
Thus, we write the final expression for the eigenvector

\displaystyle\varphi_{C^{\prime}C\;\ell_{(1)},\ell_{(3)}}(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle~{\sqrt{V\over L_{2}L_{4}}}e^{-i(x_{3}\hat{\phi}_{3}^{C^{\prime}}+x_{1}\hat{\phi}_{1}^{C^{\prime}})}\sum\limits_{m,p\in{\mathbb{Z}}}e^{i({2\pi x_{2}\over L_{2}}+L_{1}\hat{\phi}_{1}^{C^{\prime}})(m+{2C^{\prime}-1-k\over 2k})+i({2\pi x_{4}\over L_{4}}+\ell L_{3}\hat{\phi}_{3}^{C^{\prime}})(p-{2C-1-\ell\over 2\ell})}

\displaystyle\times~e^{-i(m+{C^{\prime}\over k}-{1+k\over 2k})\pi(1-k)+i\pi{1-\ell\over\ell}(C-1-p\ell)}\;h_{\ell_{(1)}}(x_{1}-{L_{1}L_{2}\hat{\phi}_{2}^{C^{\prime}}\over 2\pi}-L_{1}(m+{C^{\prime}\over k}-{1+k\over 2k}))

\displaystyle\times~\;h_{\ell_{(3)}}(x_{3}-{\ell L_{3}L_{4}\hat{\phi}_{4}^{C^{\prime}}\over 2\pi}+L_{3}(C-p\ell-{1+\ell\over 2}))~.

This expression for
\varphi_{C^{\prime}C;\ell_{(1)},\ell_{(3)}}
is used to construct the propagator.

Final form of the propagator in the

\displaystyle\sum_{C^{\prime},\alpha}\;(\phi^{\alpha}_{C^{\prime}\ell_{(1)}\ell_{(3)}}(x))_{ij}\;(\phi^{\alpha}_{C^{\prime}\ell_{(1)}\ell_{(3)}}(y))_{kl}

(414)

From (

), we can explicitly write:
93
93

For brevity only, we use a slightly idiosyncratic notation. The indices

\displaystyle(\phi^{\alpha}_{C^{\prime}\ell_{(1)}\ell_{(3)}})_{ij}=\alpha\delta_{iC^{\prime}}\delta_{jC}\Phi_{C^{\prime}C\ell_{(1)}\ell_{(3)}}+\alpha^{*}\delta_{iC}\delta_{jC^{\prime}}\Phi_{C^{\prime}C\ell_{(1)}\ell_{(3)}}^{*}~.

(415)

Using (

), we find, recalling that
\sum\limits_{\alpha=1,i}\alpha^{2}=0
,

:

\displaystyle\sum_{C^{\prime},\alpha}\;(\phi^{\alpha}_{C^{\prime}\ell_{(1)}\ell_{(3)}}(x))_{ij}\;(\phi^{\alpha}_{C^{\prime}\ell_{(1)}\ell_{(3)}}(y))_{kl}

(416)

\displaystyle=2\left(\delta_{iC^{\prime}}\delta_{jC}\Phi_{C^{\prime}C\ell_{(1)}\ell_{(3)}}(x)\;\delta_{kD}\delta_{lC^{\prime}}\Phi^{*}_{C^{\prime}D\ell_{(1)}\ell_{(3)}}(y)+\delta_{iC}\delta_{jC^{\prime}}\Phi^{*}_{C^{\prime}C\ell_{(1)}\ell_{(3)}}(x)\;\delta_{kC^{\prime}}\delta_{lD}\Phi_{C^{\prime}D\ell_{(1)}\ell_{(3)}}(y)\right)

\displaystyle={1\over V}\left(\delta_{iC^{\prime}}\delta_{jC}\varphi_{C^{\prime}C\ell_{(1)}\ell_{(3)}}(x)\;\delta_{kD}\delta_{lC^{\prime}}\varphi^{*}_{C^{\prime}D\ell_{(1)}\ell_{(3)}}(y)+\delta_{iC}\delta_{jC^{\prime}}\varphi^{*}_{C^{\prime}C\ell_{(1)}\ell_{(3)}}(x)\;\delta_{kC^{\prime}}\delta_{lD}\varphi_{C^{\prime}D\ell_{(1)}\ell_{(3)}}(y)\right),

with normalization (

).

From this equation and the

, with

given in
(

), and
\varphi

). We stress that

To compute the

), the

\displaystyle\sum_{C^{\prime},\alpha}\;\sigma_{\mu\gamma\dot{\gamma}}D_{\mu}(\phi^{\alpha}_{C^{\prime}\ell_{(1)}\ell_{(3)}}(x))_{ij}\;\bar{\sigma}_{\nu}^{\dot{\gamma}\beta}D_{\nu}(\phi^{\alpha}_{C^{\prime}\ell_{(1)}\ell_{(3)}}(y))_{kl}

Appendix G
Expressions for open Wilson lines to order
\Delta^{0}

In the
SU(k)
space, the nonvanishing components of the open Wilson lines
{\cal W}_{\mu}
(
101
) entering the gauge invariant correllators of section
2.4
are:

\displaystyle||{\cal W}_{1C^{\prime}B^{\prime}}||(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\exp\left[i2\pi x_{1}\left(-\ell\frac{z_{1}}{L_{1}}I_{k}+\frac{\bm{a}_{1}\cdot\bm{H}_{(k)}}{L_{1}}\right)\right],

\displaystyle||{\cal W}_{2C^{\prime}B^{\prime}}||(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\exp\left\{i2\pi x_{2}\left[-\ell\left(\frac{z_{2}}{L_{2}}+\frac{x_{1}}{NL_{1}L_{2}}\right)I_{k}+\frac{\bm{a}_{2}\cdot\bm{H}_{(k)}}{L_{2}}\right]\right\},

\displaystyle||{\cal W}_{3C^{\prime}B^{\prime}}||(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\exp\left[i2\pi x_{3}\left(-\ell\frac{z_{3}}{L_{3}}I_{k}+\frac{\bm{a}_{3}\cdot\bm{H}_{(k)}}{L_{3}}\right)\right],

\displaystyle||{\cal W}_{4C^{\prime}B^{\prime}}||(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\exp\left\{i2\pi x_{4}\left[-\ell\left(\frac{z_{4}}{L_{4}}+\frac{x_{3}}{N\ell L_{3}L_{4}}\right)I_{k}+\frac{\bm{a}_{4}\cdot\bm{H}_{(k)}}{L_{4}}\right]\right\}.

(423)

Recall that
{\bm{H}}_{(k)}\equiv(H_{(k)}^{1},...,H_{(k)}^{k-1})
are the
SU(k)
Cartan generators obeying
\mathrm{tr}\,\left[{H}_{(k)}^{a}{H}_{(k)}^{b}\right]=\delta^{ab}
,
a,b=1,...,k-1
. They can be expressed as,
{H}_{(k)}^{b}
=
diag
({\nu}^{b}_{1},{\nu}^{b}_{2},...,{\nu}^{b}_{k})
, where
\bm{\nu}_{1},...,\bm{\nu}_{k}
are the weights of the fundamental representation of
SU(k)
. These are
(k-1)
-dimensional vectors that obey
\bm{\nu}_{B^{\prime}}\cdot\bm{\nu}_{C^{\prime}}=\delta_{B^{\prime}C^{\prime}}-{1\over k}
, where
B^{\prime},C^{\prime}=1,..,k
.

Similarly, in the
SU(\ell)
space, the nonvanishing components are:

\displaystyle||{\cal W}_{1CB}||(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\exp\left[i2\pi x_{1}\left(k\frac{z_{1}}{L_{1}}I_{\ell}\right)\right],

\displaystyle||{\cal W}_{2CB}||(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\exp\left\{i2\pi x_{2}\left[k\left(\frac{z_{2}}{L_{2}}+\frac{x_{1}}{NL_{1}L_{2}}\right)I_{\ell}\right]\right\},

\displaystyle||{\cal W}_{3CB}||(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\exp\left[i2\pi x_{3}\left(k\frac{z_{3}}{L_{3}}I_{\ell}\right)\right],

\displaystyle||{\cal W}_{4CB}||(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\exp\left\{i2\pi x_{4}\left[k\left(\frac{z_{4}}{L_{4}}+\frac{x_{3}}{N\ell L_{3}L_{4}}\right)I_{\ell}\right]\right\}.

(424)

We may also express
{\cal W}_{\mu}
in terms of the basis
\bm{\tilde{H}}\equiv\left(\frac{\omega}{2\pi\sqrt{k(N-k)}},\bm{H}_{(k)}\right)
. Defining

\displaystyle\tilde{\bm{a}_{1}}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\left(-2\pi\sqrt{k(N-k)}z_{1},\bm{a}_{1}\right)\,,

\displaystyle\tilde{\bm{a}_{2}}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\left(-2\pi\sqrt{k(N-k)}\left(z_{2}+\frac{x_{1}}{NL_{1}}\right),\bm{a}_{2}\right)\,,

\displaystyle\tilde{\bm{a}_{3}}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\left(-2\pi\sqrt{k(N-k)}z_{3},\bm{a}_{3}\right)\,,

\displaystyle\tilde{\bm{a}_{4}}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\left(-2\pi\sqrt{k(N-k)}\left(z_{4}+\frac{x_{3}}{N\ell L_{3}}\right),\bm{a}_{4}\right)\,,

(425)

we can write

\displaystyle{\cal W}_{\mu}(x)=\exp\left[i2\pi\tilde{\bm{a}_{\mu}}\cdot\tilde{\bm{H}}\frac{x_{\mu}}{L_{\mu}}\right]\,.

(426)

The open Wilson lines
{\cal W}_{\mu}(x)
are not gauge invariant. For Wilson lines that wind around the torus, one can define gauge-invariant Wilson lines as

W_{\mu}[A](x)=\mbox{tr}\left[e^{i\int_{0}^{L_{\mu}}\hat{A}_{\mu}(x)}\Omega_{\mu}(x)\right]\,.

(427)

To show that
W_{\mu}
is gauge invariant, we first note that the Wilson lines
{\cal W}_{\mu}(x)
and the transition functions
95
95

95

The transformation of the transition functions ensure consistency with (
21
).

\Omega_{\mu}(x)
transform under a gauge transformation
U(x)
as

\displaystyle{\cal W}^{\prime}_{\mu}(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle U(x,x_{\mu}=0){\cal W}_{\mu}(x)U^{\dagger}(x,x_{\mu}=L_{\mu})\,,

\displaystyle\Omega^{\prime}_{\mu}(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle U(x,x_{\mu}=L_{\mu})\Omega_{\mu}(x)U^{\dagger}(x,x_{\mu}=0)

(428)

which ensures gauge invariance of (
427
).
The gauge-invariant Wilson loops that wind around the four cycles,
W_{\mu}[A](x)=\mbox{tr}\left[e^{i\int_{0}^{L_{\mu}}\hat{A}_{\mu}(x)}\Omega_{\mu}(x)\right]
, are:

\displaystyle W_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-1)^{(k-1)}e^{-i2\pi(N-k)\left(z_{1}-\frac{x_{2}}{NL_{2}}\right)}\left[\sum\limits_{C^{\prime}=1}^{k}e^{i2\pi\bm{a}_{1}\cdot\bm{\nu}_{C^{\prime}}}\right]+(N-k)e^{i2\pi k\left(z_{1}-\frac{x_{2}}{NL_{2}}\right)}\,,

\displaystyle W_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{-i2\pi(N-k)\left(z_{2}+\frac{x_{1}}{NL_{1}}\right)}\;\left[\sum\limits_{C^{\prime}=1}^{k}e^{i2\pi(\bm{a}_{2}-{\bm{\rho}\over k})\cdot\bm{\nu}_{C^{\prime}}}\right]+(N-k)e^{i2\pi k\left(z_{2}+\frac{x_{1}}{NL_{1}}\right)}\,,

\displaystyle W_{3}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{-i2\pi(N-k)\left(z_{3}-\frac{x_{4}}{N\ell L_{4}}\right)}\left[\sum\limits_{C^{\prime}=1}^{k}e^{i2\pi\bm{a}_{3}\cdot\bm{\nu}_{C^{\prime}}}\right]+(N-k)\;e^{i2\pi k\left(z_{3}-\frac{x_{4}}{N\ell L_{4}}\right)}\,\gamma_{\ell}\,\delta_{\ell,1}\,,

\displaystyle W_{4}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle e^{-i2\pi(N-k)\left(z_{4}+\frac{x_{3}}{N\ell L_{3}}\right)}\left[\sum\limits_{C^{\prime}=1}^{k}e^{i2\pi\bm{a}_{4}\cdot\bm{\nu}_{C^{\prime}}}\right]+(N-k)\;e^{i2\pi k\left(z_{4}+\frac{x_{3}}{N\ell L_{3}}\right)}\gamma_{\ell}\;\;\delta_{\ell,1}\,.

(429)

Appendix H
\zeta
-function regularization

In this appendix, we briefly discuss the regularization of the sums in the sectors with
Q=k/N
and
Q=0
. We shall use the
\zeta
-function regularization technique.

We start with the
Q=k/N
-sector sum

\displaystyle{\cal S}=\sum_{k_{\mu}\in\frac{2\pi\mathbb{Z}_{\mu}}{L_{\mu}},\,k_{\mu}k_{\mu}\neq 0}\frac{m^{2}}{m^{2}+k_{\mu}k_{\mu}}\,.

(430)

Here, we are taking the mass to be real. In the bulk of the paper, the mass term that appears in the denominator is the absolute value
|m|^{2}
, while the mass term in the numerator is
m^{2}
, meaning that it can take complex values. However, generalizing the results of this appendix to a general complex mass can easily be achieved by tracking the phase of the mass that appears in the numerator. Also, we shall regularize the sums for arbitrary value of
m
, but at the end we investigate the sum in the leading order in
mL_{\mu}
. One of the main purpose of this appendix is to show that the regularized sum behaves as expected; in particular, we shall show that
{\cal S}\sim m^{2}L_{\mu}^{2}
in the limit
mV^{1/4}\ll 1
, and thus, in the strict limit
m=0
, the sum vanishes.

We rewrite
{\cal S}
as

\displaystyle{\cal S}=2^{4}{\cal S}_{4}+2^{3}{\cal S}_{3}+2^{2}{\cal S}_{2}+2{\cal S}_{1}\,,

(431)

where

\displaystyle{\cal S}_{4}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n_{1},..,n_{4}=1}^{\infty}\frac{m^{2}}{m^{2}+\left(\frac{2\pi n_{1}}{L_{1}}\right)^{2}+\left(\frac{2\pi n_{2}}{L_{2}}\right)^{2}+\left(\frac{2\pi n_{3}}{L_{3}}\right)^{2}+\left(\frac{2\pi n_{4}}{L_{4}}\right)^{2}}\,,

\displaystyle{\cal S}_{3}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n_{1},..,n_{3}=1}^{\infty}\frac{m^{2}}{m^{2}+\left(\frac{2\pi n_{1}}{L_{1}}\right)^{2}+\left(\frac{2\pi n_{2}}{L_{2}}\right)^{2}+\left(\frac{2\pi n_{3}}{L_{3}}\right)^{2}}+3\,\mbox{other permutations}\,,

\displaystyle{\cal S}_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n_{1},n_{2}=1}^{\infty}\frac{m^{2}}{m^{2}+\left(\frac{2\pi n_{1}}{L_{1}}\right)^{2}+\left(\frac{2\pi n_{2}}{L_{2}}\right)^{2}}+5\,\mbox{other permutations}\,,

\displaystyle{\cal S}_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n_{1}}^{\infty}\frac{m^{2}}{m^{2}+\left(\frac{2\pi n_{1}}{L_{1}}\right)^{2}}+3\,\mbox{other permutations}\,.

(432)

We are interested in the limit
\mbox{Lim}_{m\rightarrow 0}\,{\cal S}_{1,2,3,4}
, which we shall study in what follows.

To this end, we define

\displaystyle E_{Q}^{c^{2}}(s;a_{1},a_{2},...,a_{Q})\equiv\sum_{n_{1},n_{2},..,n_{Q}=1}^{\infty}\left(c^{2}+a_{1}^{2}n_{1}^{2}+a_{2}^{2}n_{2}^{2}+...+a_{Q}^{2}n_{Q}^{2}\right)^{-s}\,.

(433)

First, we consider the sum

\displaystyle E_{1}^{c^{2}}(s;1)\equiv\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}(n^{2}+c^{2})^{-s}\,,

(434)

which is given by
Elizalde:1994gf

\displaystyle E_{1}^{c^{2}}(s;1)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}c^{-2s}+\frac{\sqrt{\pi}}{2\Gamma(s)}|c|^{1-2s}\left[\Gamma\left(s-\frac{1}{2}\right)+4\sum_{p=1}\left(\pi p|c|\right)^{s-\frac{1}{2}}K_{s-1/2}\left(2\pi p|c|\right)\right]\,.

Particularly important series are those with
s=1
and
s=1/2
. The series
E_{1}^{c^{2}}(1;1)
is convergent, with the sum given by

\displaystyle E_{1}^{c^{2}}(1;1)=\frac{-1+c\pi\coth c\pi}{2c^{2}}\,,

(436)

and we have

\displaystyle\mbox{lim}_{|c|\rightarrow 0}c^{2}E_{1}^{c^{2}}(1;1)\approx\frac{\pi^{2}c^{2}}{6}\,.

(437)

The series
E_{1}^{c^{2}}(1/2;1)
has a pole at
s=1/2
, removing it we end up with

\displaystyle E_{1}^{c^{2}}(1/2;1)=-\frac{1}{2|c|}-\frac{1}{2}\left(\gamma+2\log|c|+\psi\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)\right)+2\sum_{p=1}^{\infty}K_{0}(2\pi p|c|)\,.

(438)

This series converges well for values of
|c|\gtrsim 1
. However, the series does not converge well in the limit
|c|\rightarrow 0
. This is, however, the limit we are interested in. Nevertheless, it is possible to study the series numerically to find that

\displaystyle\mbox{lim}_{|c|\rightarrow 0}E_{1}^{c^{2}}(1/2;1)\approx\gamma\,,

(439)

where
\gamma=0.5772
is Euler’s constant,
a result that is
|c|
-independent. Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\mbox{lim}_{|c|\rightarrow 0}c^{2}E_{1}^{c^{2}}(1/2;1)\approx\gamma c^{2}\,.

(440)

Next, we consider the sum

\displaystyle E_{2}^{c^{2}}(s;a_{1},a_{2})\equiv\sum_{n_{1},n_{2}=1}^{\infty}\left(c^{2}+a_{1}^{2}n_{1}^{2}+a_{2}^{2}n_{2}^{2}\right)^{-s}\,.

(441)

Applying (
H
) twice we obtain

\displaystyle E_{2}^{c^{2}}(s;a_{1},a_{2})=-\frac{a_{2}^{-2s}}{2}E_{1}^{c^{2}/a_{2}^{2}}(s;1)+\frac{a_{2}^{1-2s}}{a_{1}}\frac{\sqrt{\pi}\Gamma\left(s-\frac{1}{2}\right)}{2\Gamma(s)}E_{1}^{c^{2}/a_{2}^{2}}\left(s-\frac{1}{2};1\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 2\frac{a_{1}^{-2s}\sqrt{\pi}}{\Gamma(s)}\sum_{n_{2},p=1}(\pi p)^{s-1/2}\left[\frac{c^{2}}{a_{1}^{2}}+\frac{a_{2}^{2}n_{2}^{2}}{a_{1}^{2}}\right]^{-s/2+1/4}K_{s-1/2}\left\{2\pi p\sqrt{\frac{c^{2}}{a_{1}^{2}}+\frac{a_{2}^{2}n_{2}^{2}}{a_{1}^{2}}}\right\}\,.

We are interested in
E_{2}^{c^{2}}(1;a_{1},a_{2})
in the limit
|c|\rightarrow 0
. From our previous discussions, the first two terms give a constant. Also, notice that the third term in the expression of
E_{2}^{c^{2}}(1;a_{1},a_{2})
yields a constant in this limit, since the double sum starts at
n_{2}=p=1
. Thus, we find

\displaystyle\mbox{lim}_{|c|\rightarrow 0}\,c^{2}E_{2}^{c^{2}}(1;a_{1},a_{2})=

\displaystyle c^{2}\left(-\frac{\pi^{2}}{12a_{2}^{2}}+\frac{\pi\gamma}{2a_{1}a_{2}}+2\frac{\sqrt{\pi}}{a_{1}^{2}}\sum_{n_{2},p=1}(\pi p)^{1/2}\left(\frac{a_{2}n_{2}}{a_{1}}\right)^{-1/2}K_{1/2}\left(2\pi p\frac{a_{2}n_{2}}{a_{1}}\right)\right)\,.

(443)

We can further repeat the exercise for
E_{3}^{c^{2}}(s;a_{1},a_{2},a_{3})
defined via

\displaystyle E_{3}^{c^{2}}(s;a_{1},a_{2},a_{3})\equiv\sum_{n_{1},n_{2},n_{3}=1}^{\infty}\left(c^{2}+a_{1}^{2}n_{1}^{2}+a_{2}^{2}n_{2}^{2}+a_{3}^{2}a_{3}^{2}\right)^{-s}\,.

(444)

We find

\displaystyle E_{3}^{c^{2}}(s;a_{1},a_{2},a_{3})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{a_{3}^{-2s}}{4}E_{1}^{c^{2}/a_{3}^{2}}(s;1)-\frac{a_{3}^{1-2s}}{a_{2}}\frac{\sqrt{\pi}\Gamma\left(s-\frac{1}{2}\right)}{4\Gamma(s)}E_{1}^{c^{2}/a_{3}^{2}}\left(s-\frac{1}{2};1\right)

(445)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{a_{3}^{1-2s}}{a_{1}}\frac{\sqrt{\pi}\Gamma\left(s-\frac{1}{2}\right)}{4\Gamma(s)}E_{1}^{c^{2}/a_{3}^{2}}\left(s-\frac{1}{2};1\right)+\frac{a_{3}^{-2s+2}}{a_{1}a_{2}}\frac{\pi\Gamma(s-1)}{4\Gamma(s)}E_{1}^{c^{2}/a_{3}^{2}}\left(s-1;1\right)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{a_{2}^{-2s}\sqrt{\pi}}{\Gamma(s)}\sum_{n_{3},p=1}(\pi p)^{s-1/2}\left[\frac{c^{2}}{a_{2}^{2}}+\frac{a_{3}^{2}n_{3}^{2}}{a_{2}^{2}}\right]^{-s/2+1/4}K_{s-1/2}\left\{2\pi p\sqrt{\frac{c^{2}}{a_{2}^{2}}+\frac{a_{3}^{2}n_{3}^{2}}{a_{2}^{2}}}\right\}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{a_{2}^{1-2s}\pi}{a_{1}\Gamma(s)}\sum_{n_{3},p=1}(\pi p)^{s-1}\left[\frac{c^{2}}{a_{2}^{2}}+\frac{a_{3}^{2}n_{3}^{2}}{a_{2}^{2}}\right]^{-s/2+1/2}K_{s-1}\left\{2\pi p\sqrt{\frac{c^{2}}{a_{2}^{2}}+\frac{a_{3}^{2}n_{3}^{2}}{a_{2}^{2}}}\right\}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 2\frac{a_{1}^{-2s}\sqrt{\pi}}{\Gamma(s)}\sum_{n_{2},n_{3},p=1}(\pi p)^{s-1/2}\left[\frac{c^{2}}{a_{1}^{2}}+\frac{a_{2}^{2}n_{2}^{2}}{a_{1}^{2}}+\frac{a_{3}^{2}n_{3}^{2}}{a_{1}^{2}}\right]^{-s/2+1/4}

\displaystyle\times K_{s-1/2}\left\{2\pi p\sqrt{\frac{c^{2}}{a_{1}^{2}}+\frac{a_{2}^{2}n_{2}^{2}}{a_{1}^{2}}+\frac{a_{3}^{2}n_{3}^{2}}{a_{1}^{2}}}\right\}\,.

Setting
s=1
, we observe that in the limit
c\rightarrow 0
,

\lim_{c\rightarrow 0}c^{2}E_{1}^{c^{2}/a_{3}^{2}}(0;1)\sim c^{4}.

Hence, the contribution from
E_{1}^{c^{2}/a_{3}^{2}}(0;1)
is subleading and can be neglected at leading order in
c
. Consequently, just as with
E_{2}^{c^{2}}(s;a_{1},a_{2})
and
E_{1}^{c^{2}}(s;a_{1})
, we find that

\lim_{c\rightarrow 0}c^{2}E_{3}^{c^{2}}(s;a_{1},a_{2},a_{3})\sim c^{2}

to leading order in
c
.

This systematic procedure can be extended to compute
E_{4}^{c^{2}}(s;a_{1},a_{2},a_{3},a_{4})
in a similar fashion. After straightforward yet tedious computations, we find

\displaystyle E_{4}^{c^{2}}(s;a_{1},a_{2},a_{3},a_{4})={\cal R}_{1}+{\cal R}_{2}+{\cal R}_{3}+{\cal R}_{4}

\displaystyle-\frac{a_{2}^{-2s}\sqrt{\pi}}{a_{4}^{-2s}\Gamma(s)}\sum_{n_{3},n_{4},p=1}(\pi p)^{s-1/2}\left[\frac{c^{2}}{a_{2}^{2}}+\frac{a_{3}^{2}n_{3}^{2}}{a_{2}^{2}}+\frac{a_{4}^{2}n_{4}^{2}}{a_{2}^{2}}\right]^{-s/2+1/4}K_{s-1/2}\left\{2\pi p\sqrt{\frac{c^{2}}{a_{2}^{2}}+\frac{a_{3}^{2}n_{3}^{2}}{a_{2}^{2}}+\frac{a_{4}^{2}n_{4}^{2}}{a_{2}^{2}}}\right\}

\displaystyle+\frac{a_{2}^{1-2s}a_{4}^{2s}\pi}{a_{1}\Gamma(s)}\sum_{n_{3},n_{4},p=1}(\pi p)^{s-1}\left[\frac{c^{2}}{a_{2}^{2}}+\frac{a_{3}^{2}n_{3}^{2}}{a_{2}^{2}}+\frac{a_{4}^{2}n_{4}^{2}}{a_{2}^{2}}\right]^{-s/2+1/2}K_{s-1}\left\{2\pi p\sqrt{\frac{c^{2}}{a_{2}^{2}}+\frac{a_{3}^{2}n_{3}^{2}}{a_{2}^{2}}+\frac{a_{4}^{2}n_{4}^{2}}{a_{2}^{2}}}\right\}

\displaystyle+2\frac{a_{1}^{-2s}a_{4}^{2s}\sqrt{\pi}}{\Gamma(s)}\sum_{n_{2},n_{3},n_{4},p=1}(\pi p)^{s-1/2}\left[\frac{c^{2}}{a_{1}^{2}}+\frac{a_{2}^{2}n_{2}^{2}}{a_{1}^{2}}+\frac{a_{3}^{2}n_{3}^{2}}{a_{1}^{2}}+\frac{a_{4}^{2}n_{4}^{2}}{a_{1}^{2}}\right]^{-s/2+1/4}

\displaystyle\times K_{s-1/2}\left\{2\pi p\sqrt{\frac{c^{2}}{a_{1}^{2}}+\frac{a_{2}^{2}n_{2}^{2}}{a_{1}^{2}}+\frac{a_{3}^{2}n_{3}^{2}}{a_{1}^{2}}+\frac{a_{4}^{2}n_{4}^{2}}{a_{1}^{2}}}\right\}\,,

(446)

where

\displaystyle{\cal R}_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{a_{4}^{-2s}}{8}E_{1}^{c^{2}/a_{4}^{2}}(s;1)+a_{3}^{-2s}\frac{\sqrt{\pi}\Gamma\left(s-\frac{1}{2}\right)}{8\Gamma(s)}E_{1}^{c^{2}/a_{4}^{2}}\left(s-\frac{1}{2};1\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{\pi}}{2\Gamma(s)}a_{3}^{-2s}\sum_{n_{4},p=1}\left[\frac{c^{2}}{a_{4}^{2}}+n_{4}^{2}\right]^{1/4-s/2}(\pi p)^{s-1/2}K_{s-1/2}\left\{2\pi p\sqrt{\frac{c^{2}}{a_{4}^{2}}+n_{4}^{2}}\right\}\,,

\displaystyle{\cal R}_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{\pi}\Gamma(s-1/2)}{8\Gamma(s)}\frac{a_{4}^{-2s+1}}{a_{2}}E_{1}^{c^{2}/a_{4}^{2}}\left(s-\frac{1}{2};1\right)-\frac{\pi\Gamma(s-1)}{8\Gamma(s)}\frac{a_{4}^{2-2s}}{a_{2}a_{3}}E^{c^{2}/a_{4}^{2}}(s-1;1)

\displaystyle-\frac{\pi}{2\Gamma(s)}\frac{a_{3}^{1-2s}}{a_{2}}\sum_{n_{4},p=1}\left[\frac{c^{2}}{a_{3}^{2}}+\frac{a_{4}^{2}n_{4}^{2}}{a_{3}^{2}}\right]^{1/2-s/2}(\pi p)^{s-1}K_{s-1}\left\{2\pi p\sqrt{\frac{c^{2}}{a_{3}^{2}}+\frac{a_{4}^{2}n_{4}^{2}}{a_{3}^{2}}}\right\}\,,

and

\displaystyle{\cal R}_{3}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{\pi}\Gamma(s-1/2)}{8\Gamma(s)}\frac{a_{4}^{-2s+1}}{a_{1}}E_{1}^{c^{2}/a_{4}^{2}}\left(s-\frac{1}{2};1\right)-\frac{\pi\Gamma(s-1)}{8\Gamma(s)}\frac{a_{4}^{2-2s}}{a_{1}a_{3}}E^{c^{2}/a_{4}^{2}}(s-1;1)

\displaystyle-\frac{\pi}{2\Gamma(s)}\frac{a_{3}^{1-2s}}{a_{1}}\sum_{n_{4},p=1}\left[\frac{c^{2}}{a_{3}^{2}}+\frac{a_{4}^{2}n_{4}^{2}}{a_{3}^{2}}\right]^{1/2-s/2}(\pi p)^{s-1}K_{s-1}\left\{2\pi p\sqrt{\frac{c^{2}}{a_{3}^{2}}+\frac{a_{4}^{2}n_{4}^{2}}{a_{3}^{2}}}\right\}\,,

\displaystyle{\cal R}_{4}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{\pi\Gamma(s-1)}{8\Gamma(s)}\frac{a_{4}^{-2s+2}}{a_{1}a_{2}}E_{1}^{c^{2}/a_{4}^{2}}(s-1;1)+\frac{\pi^{3/2}\Gamma\left(s-\frac{3}{2}\right)}{8\Gamma(s)}\frac{a_{4}^{-2s+3}}{a_{1}a_{2}a_{3}}E_{1}^{c^{2}/a_{4}^{2}}\left(s-\frac{3}{2};1\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{a_{3}^{-2s+2}}{a_{1}a_{2}}\frac{\pi^{3/2}}{2\Gamma(s)}\sum_{n_{4},p=1}\left[\frac{c^{2}}{a_{3}^{2}}+\frac{a_{4}^{2}n_{4}^{2}}{a_{3}^{2}}\right]^{1-s/2}(\pi p)^{s-3/2}K_{s-3/2}\left\{2\pi p\sqrt{\frac{c^{2}}{a_{3}^{2}}+\frac{a_{4}^{2}n_{4}^{2}}{a_{3}^{2}}}\right\}\,.

Repeating the same analysis as before, we conclude that
\lim_{c\rightarrow 0}c^{2}E_{4}^{c^{2}}(s;a_{1},a_{2},a_{3},a_{4})\sim c^{2}
.

The key takeaway is that
{\cal S}\sim{\cal O}((mV^{1/4})^{2})
to leading order in
mV^{1/4}
.

Next, we turn to the sum in
Q=0
sector

\displaystyle{\cal S}_{(Q=0)}=\sum_{k_{\mu}=\frac{2\pi\mathbb{Z}}{L_{\mu}},\bm{p}\equiv(p_{3},p_{4})\neq 0}\frac{m}{m^{2}+M_{\bm{p},k}^{2}}\,,

(449)

where

\displaystyle M_{\bm{p},k}^{2}=\left[k_{1}^{2}+k_{2}^{2}+\left(k_{3}+\frac{2\pi p_{3}}{NL_{3}}\right)^{2}+\left(k_{4}+\frac{2\pi p_{4}}{NL_{4}}\right)^{2}\right]\,,\quad p_{3},p_{4}=1,2,..,N-1\,.

(450)

We can write
{\cal S}_{(Q=0)}
as

\displaystyle{\cal S}_{(Q=0)}=2^{4}{\cal S}_{(Q=0)4}+2^{3}{\cal S}_{(Q=0)3}+2^{2}{\cal S}_{(Q=0)2}+2{\cal S}_{(Q=0)1}\,,

(451)

where

\displaystyle{\cal S}_{(Q=0)4}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n_{1},..,n_{4}=1}^{\infty}\frac{m}{m^{2}+\left(\frac{2\pi n_{1}}{L_{1}}\right)^{2}+\left(\frac{2\pi n_{2}}{L_{2}}\right)^{2}+\left(\frac{2\pi n_{3}}{NL_{3}}\right)^{2}+\left(\frac{2\pi n_{4}}{NL_{4}}\right)^{2}}\,,

\displaystyle{\cal S}_{(Q=0)3}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n_{1},..,n_{3}=1}^{\infty}\frac{m}{m^{2}+\left(\frac{2\pi n_{1}}{L_{1}}\right)^{2}+\left(\frac{2\pi n_{2}}{L_{2}}\right)^{2}+\left(\frac{2\pi n_{3}}{NL_{3}}\right)^{2}}+3\,\mbox{other permutations}\,,

\displaystyle{\cal S}_{(Q=0)2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n_{1},n_{2}=1}^{\infty}\frac{m}{m^{2}+\left(\frac{2\pi n_{1}}{L_{1}}\right)^{2}+\left(\frac{2\pi n_{2}}{L_{2}}\right)^{2}}+4\,\mbox{other permutations}\,,

\displaystyle{\cal S}_{(Q=0)1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n_{1}}^{\infty}\frac{m}{m^{2}+\left(\frac{2\pi n_{1}}{L_{1}}\right)^{2}}+1\,\mbox{other permutation}\,.

(452)

Comparing (
H
) with (
H
), we see that the sums
{\cal S}_{(Q=0)2}
and
{\cal S}_{(Q=0)1}
in (
H
) do not include the permutations that set both
p_{1}=p_{2}=0
. We remind the reader that the condition
\bm{p}\neq 0
is vital to ensure that the algebra of the fermions is in
su(N)
. Moreover, comparing the sums (
H
) with (
H
), we find that the latter can be obtained from the former by sending
L_{3}\rightarrow NL_{3}
and
L_{4}\rightarrow NL_{4}
, keeping
L_{1},L_{2}
intact, as well as eliminating one power of the mass in the numerators of (
H
). At the end, we conclude that
{\cal S}_{(Q=0)}\sim{\cal O}(mV^{1/2})
to leading order in
mV^{1/4}
. The exact numerical value of the coefficients can be readily extracted from
E_{Q}^{c^{2}}(1,a_{1},..,a_{Q})
.

In the remainder of this appendix, we compute the leading-order correction to the partition function in the
Q=0
sector, beyond the Witten index
N
, as a small mass is introduced. In this sector, the partition function is given by the ratio between the fermion and the gauge-boson determinants:

\displaystyle Z_{Q=0}=N\frac{\prod_{k_{\mu},\bm{p}\neq\bm{0}}\left[|m|^{2}+M_{\bm{p},k}^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\prod_{k_{\mu},\bm{p}\neq\bm{0}}\left[M_{\bm{p},k}^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}}\,.

(453)

Consider the product

\displaystyle P=\prod_{k_{\mu},\bm{p}\neq\bm{0}}\left[|m|^{2}+M_{\bm{p},k}^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}\rightarrow 2\log P=\sum_{k_{\mu},\bm{p}\neq\bm{0}}\log\left[|m|^{2}+M_{\bm{p},k}^{2}\right]\,,

(454)

from which we find

\displaystyle 2\frac{\partial\log P}{\partial|m|^{2}}=\sum_{k_{\mu},\bm{p}\neq\bm{0}}\frac{1}{|m|^{2}+M_{\bm{p},k}^{2}}=\frac{{\cal S}_{(Q=0)}}{|m|}\,,

(455)

and in the limit
|m|V^{1/4}\ll 1

\displaystyle 2\frac{\partial\log P}{\partial|m|^{2}}\sim 2cV^{1/2}\rightarrow P=d\exp\left[c|m|^{2}V^{1/2}\right]\,,

(456)

where
c,d
are constants. The constant
c
can be determined exactly from the regularized sums obtained above, while
d
is a constant of integration.
Then, in the limit
|m|V^{1/4}\ll 1
, we find

\displaystyle Z_{Q=0}=N\exp\left[c|m|^{2}V^{1/2}\right]\approx N+Nc|m|^{2}V^{1/2}\,.

(457)

This result matches the calculations in the Hamiltonian formalism.
